# Cody's Cookbook — Changelog

Every change to [`codys-cookbook.md`](codys-cookbook.md) gets a line here, newest first.
Split out of the master file on 2026-09-08 at Cody's direction, once the cookbook passed 4 MB;
see CLAUDE.md §2. Nothing was edited in the move — the rows below are the cookbook's own,
in the order they stood.

[↑ Back to the cookbook](codys-cookbook.md#table-of-contents)

| Date | Change |
|---|---|
| 2026-09-09 | **[§5.63 Simple Romaine, Homemade Caesar](codys-cookbook.md#563-simple-romaine-homemade-caesar) — the mayonnaise question answered as far as the book can answer it.** Cody asked whether Prudhomme or *Salt, Fat, Acid, Heat* has a homemade mayo. **Both do, and both are already transcribed:** [§4.159 Rustic Mayonnaise](codys-cookbook.md#4159-rustic-mayonnaise) (Nosrat — one yolk, ¾ cup oil, by hand; the base §4.134 Creamy Caesar Dressing is built on) and [§4.187 Homemade Mayonnaise](codys-cookbook.md#4187-homemade-mayonnaise) (Prudhomme — whole egg, 1½ cups oil, cider vinegar, white pepper, in a blender). The open question now names both and states the consequence plainly: **both are raw-egg mayonnaises and both print roughly 3 days**, so a homemade base of either kind gives the dressing a 3-day life, and his *"keeps a week"* only holds on a jarred base. The fork — homemade or jarred — is still his to call. |
| 2026-09-09 | **Stage C of the late dictation — four new recipes, each with the full §3 structure, a TOC line, a Difficulty & Time Index row, and a line on the [Meal Planning Sheet](codys-cookbook.md#meal-planning-sheet) sorted into its group by minutes.** **① [§8.47 Frito Pie](codys-cookbook.md#847-frito-pie) ❤️** — Cody's: *"Fritos, and then you put chili on it, and then shredded cheese, and then raw onions, and then some Louisiana hot sauce on the top of there — watch out, buddy, it is delicious. One of my favorite things."* The order is his and is the recipe; quantities are cookbook judgment reasoned from [§8.1 Chili Mac](codys-cookbook.md#81-chili-mac)'s own bowl (Frito-Lay's printed version is a baked casserole and was not used). **Built on leftover chili, and the chili recipe still does not exist** — written against "chili" generically, cross-linked to §8.1's missing-sibling note and the [§12](codys-cookbook.md#12-recipes-to-document) row (both now name it), and it links up the day his chili lands; until then §8.1's crock-pot chili without the noodles stands in. 🟢 · Prep ~10 · Cook ~5. On the sheet's 🍱 Leftovers group as *Leftover Chili → Frito Pie* ❤️ 🟩(10 min) 🍽️, sorted at 10 beside Chili Mac's 15; the "entry coming" note on the Chili Mac line and the preamble's "still to be written" both replaced. Family Ratings Index: Soups & chili line, 85 → 86 hearts. Open: which Louisiana hot sauce bottle, which cheese. **② [§6.11 Cody's Tony Chachere's Smoked Turkey](codys-cookbook.md#611-codys-tony-chacheres-smoked-turkey) ❤️** — *"one of the best in the world."* Products confirmed by him (ADDENDUM item 4): **Tony Chachere's Creole Style Butter Injectable Marinade** for the injection, *"a whole bunch"* (Tony's own direction: *use as much as you'd like, space the sites*), and **Tony Chachere's Original Creole Seasoning** as the rub, *liberally* — *"two different products, same company."* Night before: inject, rub, dry-brine overnight **upright on a cavity stand** (*"not beer-can — a rack, a thing that goes inside its butt and it sits upright"*), and it stays upright through the smoke. **Tony's is salted, so [§6.1](codys-cookbook.md#61-smoked-brisket)'s Path B rule applies — no kosher salt underneath** — cross-linked to [§T37](codys-cookbook.md#t37-salt-brining-and-the-dry-brine--codys-standing-practice-heavy-every-time) and [§7.9](codys-cookbook.md#79-caveman-chicken-wings)'s identical rule, with a labeled caution that a processor-salted bird ([§T80](codys-cookbook.md#t80-choosing-your-turkey)) would be a third dose. **The Egg temperature he forgot (*"three fifty, three seventy-five, something like that"*) is filled under §3d at 325°F indirect, from Big Green Egg's own published whole-bird *Smoked Turkey* recipe (325°F, convEGGtor, ~12 min/lb; its spatchcocked recipe is 350°F, likely the number he half-remembers), agreeing with [§T81](codys-cookbook.md#t81-cooking-the-perfect-turkey)'s *as close to 325°F as your cooker will let you — this crisps the skin*; labeled, no URL (the pages could not be opened from this environment), his number supersedes.** Pull at 160°F breast is his own standing rule from [§7.122](codys-cookbook.md#7122-the-ultimate-smoked-turkey), applied here; a side-by-side table with §7.122 as the Meathead counterpart; [§T35](codys-cookbook.md#t35-smoke-science--combustion-the-smoke-ring-and-getting-blue-smoke)'s palmful rule for wood; [§T67](codys-cookbook.md#t67-ban-the-v-shaped-rack)/§T81 explain why the stand is sound (air on every side, open cavity). 🟢 Easy (nothing goes from perfect to ruined on a probe; §7.122's 🟡 came from its gravy and hot-bird handoff, neither here) · Prep ~45 · Slow cook ~2½–3½ hr · ~1 day. Sheet: 🐔 Chicken & Poultry, 🟨(75 min, incl. 30 grill) ♨︎, sorted after Caveman Wings' 75† on the ❤️ tiebreak. Family Ratings: Smoked meats line, now 7 of 11 in §6, 86 → 87 hearts. Injection, stand and injector on the grocery/equipment list, orderable online per §3e. Open: his temperature, which Egg and whether a 14-lb bird fits upright under the dome, how much injectable, the wood, the bird's size and whether it is unsalted. **③ [§4.196 Fresh Cranberry Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#4196-fresh-cranberry-sauce) 🦃** — *"Frozen cranberries — follow the recipe on the label. Sugar and water, boil it, then put it in the fridge. Super duper easy."* Quantities filled under §3d from the bag label — Ocean Spray's own printed *Fresh Cranberry Sauce*: 1 cup sugar, 1 cup water, one 12 oz bag (fresh or frozen), boil gently 10 min, cool, refrigerate, makes 2¼ cups — labeled as the label's, his to supersede. 🟢 · Prep ~5 · Cook ~15. Filed in §4 as a condiment; **closes the [§5.24 Cranberry Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#524-cranberry-sauce) placeholder**, which now points there (TOC line annotated, stub kept as a pointer). Sheet: Sauces block, Fresh & cold group, a new **Holiday** row 🟩(15 min) with the group intro amended for the one sauce there that touches a stove — the 🦃 holiday sides sit in ordinary groups without a sheet marker, matching [§5.11](codys-cookbook.md#511-sweet-potato-casserole). **④ [§5.63 Simple Romaine, Homemade Caesar](codys-cookbook.md#563-simple-romaine-homemade-caesar)** — the third salad on the rotation with [§5.61](codys-cookbook.md#561-simple-romaine-or-butter-lettuce-salad-with-grana-padano) and [§5.23](codys-cookbook.md#523-greek-salad-with-crumbled-goat-cheese): romaine, [§4.134 Creamy Caesar Dressing](codys-cookbook.md#4134-creamy-caesar-dressing) (built on [§4.159](codys-cookbook.md#4159-rustic-mayonnaise) or store-bought), a wedge of **Grana Padano or Romano — *"not American Parmesan… even if we have to order it"*** (§5.61's explainer cross-linked, not repeated; a labeled Romano note added; orderable online per §3e), croutons optional (§4.134's own torn-crouton note reused). **Keeping, recorded both ways:** Cody says one batch keeps about a week and covers several nights; **§4.134 itself prints *up to 3 days*, and so does §4.159 under it** — both figures stand in the entry, the source's flagged as the cautious raw-yolk number, his as his; Open Question. 🟢 · Prep ~10 (+ §4.134's 15 the night the dressing is made). **Sheet: the Stage A placeholder line (plain text pointing at §4.134, "entry coming") replaced with a real link to §5.63**, 🟩(10 min), and the preamble note updated. **Census, before → after:** table-row recipe-anchor links on the sheet **445 → 449, exactly the four new lines, and the set diff shows only the four new anchors — nothing lost**; whole-section links 633 → 639 (the four rows plus the two updated preamble notes). Unicode-aware link check (Python `re`, `\w`) across all three files clean at every commit, and proven non-vacuous with an injected-then-reverted bogus anchor. Four commits, one per recipe. |

The 2026-09-09 open question closed. The two entries that cited Darcy's as the 🟡 reference ([§6.10](codys-cookbook.md#610-sous-vide-brisket-richies-method), [§7.151](codys-cookbook.md#7151-sous-vide-chicken-thighs-for-the-family-seared)) now say so in the past tense; §7.151 and §T28 keep their own 🟡 — his ruling was about the steak. **⑦ ADDENDUM item 5 — the meal calendar logged in [§12](codys-cookbook.md#12-recipes-to-document) as a wanted future build:** a per-dinner event that back-schedules the start from the sheet's hands-on minutes, each entry's Slow cook hours, and its lead time — *"especially important for sous vide and grilling."* Not built; the row is the specification. **⑧ Factual correction on [§7.107 Skinny Steaks](codys-cookbook.md#7107-skinny-steaks):** its 2026-09-09 note had called [§7.132 Kōji Filet Mignon](codys-cookbook.md#7132-kōji-filet-mignon) "genuinely sous vide." It is a kōji marinade and a hot sear with no water bath anywhere in its method (the entry itself was checked and carries no stray sous vide language); the note now names only [§7.131](codys-cookbook.md#7131-smoked-sous-vide-steak). **Two things this pass could not settle from the entries alone:** [§8.0 The Hearty Main Course Soup Formula](codys-cookbook.md#80-the-hearty-main-course-soup-formula) has no method of its own (30 is the formula's own sauté-and-load), and [§5.12 Southern Cornbread Stuffing](codys-cookbook.md#512-southern-cornbread-stuffing)'s 25 assumes the cornbread is on hand — baking it for the dressing is [§15.8](codys-cookbook.md#158-old-fashioned-skillet-cornbread-and-hush-puppies)'s own line. Stage C (the four new recipes) is a separate pass. |
| 2026-09-09 | **The late dictation — a full QC of the [Meal Planning Sheet](codys-cookbook.md#meal-planning-sheet)'s maintenance-time squares, and the page rebuilt around the result. This is Stage A of three: the rules, the structure, and Cody's own figures. The line-by-line re-estimate of every other square (Stage B) and the four new recipes — Frito Pie, Tony Chachere's Smoked Turkey, Fresh Cranberry Sauce, Simple Romaine with Homemade Caesar (Stage C) — follow in their own passes.** **① The square's rule is rewritten, three ways.** *Hands-on time only* — *"if you have to bake it in the oven, you don't count baking in the oven, because that's just waiting" · "soup simmering should not count" · "just do the prep time"* — so unattended bakes and simmers of any length are out, not just Slow cook. **+30 min grill overhead on any ♨︎ main or appetizer** (*"overriding anything that I just said: a grill is an extra thirty minutes"*), **none on grilled sides** (*"I'm only gonna be doing grilled sides while I'm doing a grilled meat"*), **+10 for a pan-seared finish** (*"if you're searing, that takes an extra ten minutes"*), **neither for leftovers.** And **his own figures override everything** — marked **†** on the sheet, never given overhead on top, never re-derived. The legend says outright that this is narrower than [CLAUDE.md §3a](CLAUDE.md)'s Cook field and that the entry badges and the [Difficulty & Time Index](codys-cookbook.md#difficulty--time-index) are unchanged. **② New sort order: time ascending is the primary key inside every group; ❤️ then 👍 only break ties** (*"the quickest pork main dish would be at the top of the pork category… in time order, least amount of time first"*). Applied to every group in both columns, the appetizer block, and the rub and sauce rows; the French mother-sauce table and rows led by *(the parent)* keep their parent-first order; a range sorts and colours on its low end; his five earlier pins now sort by time like everything else and the page says so, for him to reverse. **③ Forty-six of his figures applied, ranges kept as ranges:** Hamburgers with Herb Butter 50–55 · Smoked Brisket 60–90 · Sous Vide Brisket 50 · Herb-Crusted Tenderloin 45 · Steakhouse Steakburgers 50 · Mamma's Deer/Beef Stew 40 · Skinny Steaks 50 · Rolled Flank Steak 50 · Skirt Steak 60 · Sous Vide Smoked Steak 35–40 · Cody's Pulled Pork Sandwiches 40 (*"at least"*) · Sous Vide Chicken 20–30 · Sous Vide Chicken Thighs 30 · Bob Gibson's 55 · Caveman Wings 75 · Honey Chicken Kabobs 70 · Grilled Stuffed Chicken Breasts 50 · Ultimate Smoked Turkey 45 · Salmon with Mustard Glaze 45 · Paul's Jambalaya 30 · Lemon Chicken Soup 30 · Italian Sausage & Kale Soup 40 · Sweet Potato & Andouille Soup 60 · Tomato Basil Soup 60 · Chicken Stock 15 · Restorative Beef Broth 15 · Keto Egg & Sausage Muffins 30 · Loaded Frittata 30 · Soft Scrambled Eggs 35 · Frank's Buffalo Chicken Dip 30 · Beef Jerky 60 · Deer Jerky 60 · Char-Grilled Oysters 120 · Easy Deviled Eggs 15 · Deviled Eggs 60 · Sautéed Asparagus 13 · Mamma's Spinach 13 · Lemon Green Beans 15 · Grilled Cauliflower 15–20 · Grilled Asparagus 15 · Southern Fauxtato Salad 30 · Heather's Mashed Potatoes 60 · Brazilian Black Beans 15 · Simple Romaine 10 · Greek Salad 15 · Darcy's Steak 35 (*"about right"*) — plus Leftover Street Tacos 20 on its new line, and Baba Ghanoush +30 on his instruction. Grill overhead went onto 71 ♨︎ lines and sear overhead onto the five sous vide dishes that finish in a skillet — [§7.78](codys-cookbook.md#778-boar-tenderloin-with-cherry-chutney), [§7.81](codys-cookbook.md#781-scallops-with-quick-pickled-vegetables-and-pomegranate-reduction), [§7.83](codys-cookbook.md#783-short-rib-korean-style-lettuce-wraps), [§7.84](codys-cookbook.md#784-rockfish-burger-with-key-lime-aioli), [§7.85](codys-cookbook.md#785-hanger-steak-with-duck-fat-wild-mushrooms). The other 305 squares are untouched Prep + Cook figures awaiting the Stage B re-read. Colour buckets now stand at 🟩 249 · 🟨 121 · 🟥 65. **④ Seven structural changes.** A new **🍱 Leftovers** group at the foot of the mains column — sliced brisket reheated in the sous vide, pulled pork sandwiches, street tacos, kebabs, leftover chili into [§8.1 Chili Mac](codys-cookbook.md#81-chili-mac) (Frito Pie to follow), and [§7.146](codys-cookbook.md#7146-pho-with-leftover-brisket-and-smoked-bone-broth) retitled on the sheet to *Pho with Leftover Beef* — **no ♨︎ on any of it** (*"all leftovers does not need the grill"*), pointed at [§T61](codys-cookbook.md#t61-leftover-pulled-pork--storage-and-reheating) and [§T46](codys-cookbook.md#t46-freezing-and-reheating-leftovers); the "Sliced Smoked Brisket" line that confused him (*"I don't understand that… there's smoked brisket"*) is plain **Smoked Brisket** now, the cook. **"Sous Vide" prefixed to fifteen more sheet titles** whose method is sous vide — [Cody's Pho](codys-cookbook.md#1-codys-pho) included on his say-so, [Fennel Fondant](codys-cookbook.md#539-fennel-fondant) in the sides — making eighteen with the three that already carried it; [§7.132 Kōji Filet Mignon](codys-cookbook.md#7132-kōji-filet-mignon) is *not* prefixed, because it is a marinade and a grill, not a bath. **🦌 Game & Wild Birds dissolved** (*"I wouldn't put game and wild birds"*): [§7.67](codys-cookbook.md#767-grilled-quail-with-pear-and-pine-nut-salad) to Chicken & Poultry as *Grilled Quail (or chicken thighs)*, [§7.78](codys-cookbook.md#778-boar-tenderloin-with-cherry-chutney) to Pork as *Sous Vide Boar Tenderloin (or pork tenderloin)*. **Four renames on the sheet, entries untouched**: [§7.36](codys-cookbook.md#736-two-steaks-feed-four) *Steaks That Feed a Family of Five*; **fresh shrimp a bolded requirement** in [§7.22 Captain Mike's Ceviche](codys-cookbook.md#722-captain-mikes-ceviche)'s ingredients and grocery list (*"Fresh shrimp is absolutely necessary — put that on there"*). **[§7.31 Salmon with Mustard Glaze](codys-cookbook.md#731-salmon-with-mustard-glaze)** gained an optional [§T17](codys-cookbook.md#t17-the-raw-egg-soak-deodorizing-fish--shellfish) egg soak **with the skin kept on** (*"he actually sears the skin to get it crispy"*) — the skin-off order [§7.150](codys-cookbook.md#7150-grilled-salmon-with-avocado-salsa) follows explicitly does not apply, and §T17 §C2 records the exception. **⑤ His batching note** on [§1](codys-cookbook.md#1-codys-pho) and [§7.146](codys-cookbook.md#7146-pho-with-leftover-brisket-and-smoked-bone-broth): *"sometimes when I sous vide a beef I'll do two or three chuck roasts and reuse it when I make pho."* **Still his to answer:** which championship pork gets his 45 — [§7.137 the butt](codys-cookbook.md#7137-tuffy-stones-championship-pork-butt) or [§7.136 the ribs](codys-cookbook.md#7136-championship-pork-ribs); it now names this one**, and §T28 carries a pointer forward. **He considered a quartered whole chicken and chose thighs — recorded as his call**, with the reason written up: dark meat runs about 10 percent fat against a breast's 6 ([§T77](codys-cookbook.md#t77-tips-on-cooking-poultry)), so it is forgiving at 148°F and holds texture over a long hold, where breast dries and a quartered bird puts both in one bag. **Three things are cookbook judgment and are labeled as such in the entry** per [CLAUDE.md §3d](CLAUDE.md): **boneless skin-on thighs** — reasoned from this cookbook first, since [§T28](codys-cookbook.md#t28-sous-vide-chicken-codys-method)'s sear is a flat-contact sear and [§T45](codys-cookbook.md#t45-bones--do-they-actually-add-flavor) says both that bones add no flavor in dry cooking and that the goal is *"golden-to-dark brown across as much surface as possible — not stripes"*; The realistic field is 🔥, 🍖, 🥩, 🍢, ♨︎ and 🍳, and he picked **hot springs**. **"Black" means the text-presentation variant, not the colour emoji** — the character is written as **U+2668 followed by VARIATION SELECTOR-15 (U+FE0E)**, which requests monochrome rendering, rather than U+FE0F which requests the colour glyph. **All 103 markers inside the sheet were swapped; the 5 🔥 elsewhere in the cookbook are unrelated and untouched** (§3's two-cooler callout among them), and the legend and the three prose sentences that said "flame" were reworded. **One limitation worth recording: VS15 is a request, not a guarantee** — a renderer is free to show colour anyway, and several do. | 2026-09-09 | **Darcy's second steak method, the 🔥 outdoor icon on the [Meal Planning Sheet](codys-cookbook.md#meal-planning-sheet), and a four-way ratings audit.** **[§3 Darcy's Steak](codys-cookbook.md#3-darcys-steak) now documents two methods.** Her **stovetop reverse sear** is written up as a variation inside §3 — [§3.D](codys-cookbook.md#d-variation--darcys-stovetop-reverse-sear-the-weeknight-method) — not as a new numbered entry, because it is the same cook's steak, the same [§4.1 Darcy's Steak Rub](codys-cookbook.md#41-darcys-steak-rub), the same pat-dry rule and the same **129°F** target. Slow in a pan to 129°F, **the whole batch to temperature and set aside before anything is seared** — *"she cooks all the steaks to 129"* — then pat dry, avocado oil both sides, and sear in that same pan. Cody: *"It's pretty much reverse seared — reverse pan seared steak… That's a simple thing, and it's good."* §3's headnote is rewritten to open with both methods and when to pick each: the reverse sear needs no bath, no bags and no ice cooler, so it is the weeknight version, while the bath is what tenderizes a cheap cut and the ice chill is what buys the darker crust. **Pat dry is drawn as the explicit through-line** — the same instruction that earned the dedicated black towel. **Rated 🟡 Medium, judged on its own and compared against the sous vide version in the entry:** a pan can be walked past 129°F where a water bath physically cannot, and the sear still has no doneness cushion — but a pan is hundreds of degrees cooler than a chimney, so the two risks roughly trade places. Not 🔴 (nothing fails inside a minute), not 🟢 (a temperature you can overshoot is what 🟢 excludes), and its speed is no more a difficulty input than length is. **⚠️ The ordering is flagged, not silently resolved:** Cody corrected himself mid-sentence about pat-dry versus rub versus oil, so §3.D is written **pat dry → season → cook to 129°F → pat dry again → oil → sear**, that ordering is labeled in the entry as **the cookbook's reading** (reasoned from §3's own sous vide steps and [§T41 Reverse Sear](codys-cookbook.md#t41-reverse-sear--two-stage-cooking), per CLAUDE.md §3d), and it heads §3's Open Questions along with which cut the pan version wants, the burner setting, the sear time in seconds, and what happens to a large batch. §3's badge now carries a line per method, the [Difficulty & Time Index](codys-cookbook.md#difficulty--time-index) has a second row for §3.D (🟡 · prep 10 min · cook 45 min · ~55 min), and the Table of Contents line says there are two methods. **🔥 on the planning sheet, at Cody's request** — *"a little grill icon in anything that typically involves the grill… the grill or the smoker, or any kind of outdoor cooking."* **108 lines are marked** and the legend says plainly that **the icon reflects the recipe as written, not what is possible**: [§3](codys-cookbook.md#3-darcys-steak) earns it for the charcoal-chimney sear, and its new stovetop variation earns nothing. All of [§6 Smoked Meats](codys-cookbook.md#6-smoked-meats), the grilled vegetables in [§5](codys-cookbook.md#5-vegetables--sides) and the grilled and smoked mains in [§7](codys-cookbook.md#7-mains--proteins) qualify; I mean there's a ton of French sauces that I got and there's a ton of just wonderful sauces that can be made."* **He was right, and the count proves it:** [§4](codys-cookbook.md#4-rubs-seasonings-sauces--condiments) holds roughly **126 sauce-type entries against 31 rubs**, and the single strip carried about 35 items weighted toward the rubs — so the best-stocked part of the cookbook was the part getting one line. **The rubs block keeps what it had**, regrouped Beef / Pork / Chicken & Poultry / Seafood / Anything and widened to take the dry seasonings, brines and injections ([§4.100](codys-cookbook.md#4100-the-simple-blonder-wet-brine-63-salinity)–[§4.104](codys-cookbook.md#4104-beef-or-venison-brine-injection), [§4.171](codys-cookbook.md#4171-cool-smoke-injection)) — 33 items. **The sauces block names 98 of the ~126, in seven groups sorted by what a sauce *is* and what it goes on, not by source book:** *The French mother sauces & their children* (12 — the white family parent-first from [§4.63 Sauce Béchamel / Sauce Velouté](codys-cookbook.md#463-sauce-béchamel--sauce-velouté-master-white-sauce) down through [Mornay](codys-cookbook.md#465-sauce-mornay-cheese-sauce), [Aurore](codys-cookbook.md#466-sauce-aurore-béchamel-or-velouté-with-tomato-flavoring), [Soubise](codys-cookbook.md#469-sauce-soubise-onion-sauce) and the rest, plus the three brown bases [§4.71](codys-cookbook.md#471-sauce-brune-flour-based-brown-sauce--brown-sauce-1)–[§4.73](codys-cookbook.md#473-jus-lié-starch-thickened-brown-sauce--brown-sauce-3); the block says in one line that these are **a system, not a list**, and points at [§T24](codys-cookbook.md#t24-the-french-sauce-families--roux-ratios) as the map); *Wine & brown sauces* (12, the group Cody asked for by name — [Madère / au Porto](codys-cookbook.md#480-sauce-madère--sauce-au-porto-brown-madeira-or-port-wine-sauce), [Périgueux](codys-cookbook.md#481-sauce-périgueux-brown-madeira-sauce-with-truffles), [Diable](codys-cookbook.md#474-sauce-diable-peppery-brown-sauce), [Piquante](codys-cookbook.md#475-sauce-piquante-brown-sauce-with-pickles-and-capers), [Robert](codys-cookbook.md#476-sauce-robert-brown-mustard-sauce), [Duxelles](codys-cookbook.md#479-sauce-duxelles-brown-mushroom-sauce), [Sherry Wine Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#4188-sherry-wine-sauce)); *Butter & emulsion sauces* (12, with the two that genuinely break flagged 🔴 — [Hollandaise](codys-cookbook.md#49-hollandaise-sauce) and [Beurre Blanc](codys-cookbook.md#485-beurre-blanc--beurre-nantais-white-butter-sauce)); *Fish & seafood sauces* (12, the other group he named — [Shrimp and Crab Butter Cream](codys-cookbook.md#48-shrimp-and-crab-butter-cream-sauce), [Fish Brown Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#412-fish-brown-sauce) 🔴, [Czarina](codys-cookbook.md#421-czarina-sauce-with-shrimp-or-crawfish), [Remoulade](codys-cookbook.md#423-remoulade-sauce) ❤️, [Mustard-Dill](codys-cookbook.md#439-mustard-dill-sauce) ❤️, [Pecan Butter](codys-cookbook.md#424-pecan-butter-sauce)); *Barbecue & vinegar sauces* (12, grouped by what the sauce is made of, which is also by region); *Fresh & cold sauces* (30, the biggest and most curated); and *Pasta & tomato sauces* (8). **❤️ and 🔴 travel with each name** — a family verdict and a can-this-break warning are both decision-relevant when you are picking a sauce on a weeknight; 🔴 is added to the sheet's legend. **Curation, stated on the page:** where a source printed several near-identical versions of one sauce — seven vinaigrettes, four herb salsas, four pestos, three Carolina vinegar sauces, two Lexington dips — the parent is named and the rest are pointed at in a note under that group, and the page says plainly that [§4](codys-cookbook.md#4-rubs-seasonings-sauces--condiments) holds the complete list. Both blocks stay printable. **The dish-specific rule is unchanged and still excludes three sauces by name** — Alabama White Sauce is part of [§7.119](codys-cookbook.md#7119-big-bob-gibsons-chicken-in-bama-white-sauce), Avocado Salsa part of [§7.150](codys-cookbook.md#7150-grilled-salmon-with-avocado-salsa), and the Sweet and Tangy Dipping Sauce part of [§9.32](codys-cookbook.md#932-coconut-beer-shrimp-with-sweet-and-tangy-dipping-sauce), whose own page calls it *not optional to the dish*. **Three items come back onto the sheet, though, because the rule had been applied the wrong way to them** — the same error already corrected for [§4.1 Darcy's Steak Rub](codys-cookbook.md#41-darcys-steak-rub) on 2026-09-08. The test is whether a recipe **builds a sauce in**, not whether any recipe mentions it. [§4.109 Simon & Garfunkel Rub](codys-cookbook.md#4109-simon--garfunkel-rub) is *referenced* by [§7.118](codys-cookbook.md#7118-simon--garfunkel-chicken) — its ingredient line reads *2 tablespoons Simon & Garfunkel Rub (§4.109)* and its grocery list says the ingredients are already documented there, which is the Darcy's pattern exactly. [§4.23 Remoulade Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#423-remoulade-sauce) is used by **two** dishes, [§9.9](codys-cookbook.md#99-shrimp-remoulade) and [§9.10](codys-cookbook.md#910-crabmeat-avery), and carries Cody's ❤️. And [§4.18 Lemon Butter Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#418-lemon-butter-sauce) is claimed by no dish at all — its own source line says *serve this over fish, veal, beef steaks, or lamb chops*, and [§5.16](codys-cookbook.md#516-asparagus-with-lemon-butter-sauce), which sounds like it owns the name, uses [§4.56 Canal House Lemon Butter](codys-cookbook.md#456-canal-house-lemon-butter) instead. The reversal is recorded on the sheet itself, not just here. **Also updated:** the sheet's *How to use this* step 5 no longer says rubs and sauces are the last strip — it names both blocks and says why they were split. **And, logged in the same pass:** **[§12 Recipes To Document](codys-cookbook.md#12-recipes-to-document): new row for *Cook Without a Book*, a sauce source Cody named and offered on 2026-09-09** while reviewing the new Sauces block. **Searched and verified the same day: nothing from that book is in this cookbook** — no recipe, no note, no citation, and no §4 entry credited to it anywhere in `codys-cookbook.md` or `CHANGELOG.md`. **It is logged as a lead, not a photograph request** — nothing was ever shot, so nothing is missing, and §12's standing note that the table asks for no more pages is unaffected (a clarifying clause was added to that note saying so). No author, edition, or page range is asserted, because Cody has not given one. It is tracked so the source isn't lost between sessions. |
| 2026-09-08 | **Meal Planning Sheet correction — [§4.1 Darcy's Steak Rub](codys-cookbook.md#41-darcys-steak-rub) restored to the sauce strip's Beef line.** The dish-specific rule that keeps a built-in sauce off the sheet had been applied to it, on the grounds that [§3 Darcy's Steak](codys-cookbook.md#3-darcys-steak) uses it. **That was the rule applied too strictly, and two facts settle it:** §4.1's own first sentence calls it *an all-purpose steak blend*, and §3 **references** it rather than building it in — its seasoning note points at §4.1 as documentation, which is the opposite of a sauce baked into a dish's method. A rub you choose freely and use across ribeye, strip and tri-tip is exactly what the strip is for. **Cody's ❤️ carried across with it** — it's a family favorite and the one a reader would most likely go looking for. The rule itself is unchanged and the other four removals stand: Simon & Garfunkel Rub, Cowboy Java Rub, Remoulade, and Lemon Butter Sauce are each named in a specific dish's title or method. |
| 2026-09-08 | **New front-matter section — [Meal Planning Sheet](codys-cookbook.md#meal-planning-sheet), a page to print and stick on the fridge.** Cody asked for the cookbook broken down *"in an organization way that is conducive to meal planning… on the left column you have meats, and then on the right column you have sides,"* and explicitly deferred a web app: *"let's just do it the old fashioned way for right now."* So it is a **markdown two-column table** — mains flowing down the left, sides down the right — placed with the other front-matter indexes, after the [Family Ratings Index](codys-cookbook.md#family-ratings-index) and before [§1](codys-cookbook.md#1-codys-pho), with its own Table of Contents line. **Not a numbered recipe section**, because it documents nothing new; it is a way into what's already here. **Every main is named as the meal, not the ingredient**, which was his sharpest instruction: not *Pulled Pork* but [Pulled Pork Sandwiches](codys-cookbook.md#62-pulled-pork) and [Pulled Pork Street Tacos](codys-cookbook.md#71-pulled-pork-tacos), not *brisket* but [Sliced Smoked Brisket](codys-cookbook.md#61-smoked-brisket). **A dish that builds in its own sauce or topping is ONE line, sauce included, and that sauce appears nowhere else on the sheet.** His words on the case that set the rule: *"Bob Gibson's chicken with the Alabama sauce should be the main dish, and Alabama sauce should be part of the main dish, not a side condiment thing."* So [§7.119 Big Bob Gibson's Chicken in 'Bama White Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#7119-big-bob-gibsons-chicken-in-bama-white-sauce) is one main and [§4.172 Alabama White Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#4172-alabama-white-sauce) is **not selectable anywhere** — not as a side, not as a condiment, not in the closing sauce strip. Same for the avocado salsa on [§7.150](codys-cookbook.md#7150-grilled-salmon-with-avocado-salsa) and the Cowboy Java rub on [§7.100](codys-cookbook.md#7100-pork-tenderloin-with-cowboy-java-rub). **🍽️ marks the dishes that are the whole meal** — his examples were jambalaya and red beans and rice — defined on the sheet as *the starch and the vegetable are already in it*, which is why every entry in the pasta/rice and sandwich groups carries it and no beef roast does. **Soups are mains, per his instruction**, with the sheet saying to give one bread or a salad rather than treating it as a starter. **Groups:** mains in **Beef & Venison · Pork · Chicken & Poultry · Seafood & Fish · Pasta, Rice & Noodle Bowls · Sandwiches & Handhelds · Soups, Gumbos & Chili · Breakfast for Dinner**; sides in **Vegetables · Potatoes, Rice & Grains · Beans · Salads & Slaws · Breads & Biscuits · No-Cook**. Beans got their own small group rather than riding with the starches, since a bean side is chosen for different reasons than a rice. **Appetizers and dips are their own block** below the sheet, split dips-and-spreads from finger-food, as he asked; [§9.5 Frank's RedHot Buffalo Chicken Dip](codys-cookbook.md#95-franks-redhot-buffalo-chicken-dip) is the chicken dip he named. **No artichoke dip was added** — *"I don't really like artichoke dip necessarily."* **Cottage cheese is on the sheet as he asked, and honestly:** it appears in the **No-Cook Sides** group as a plain pantry item marked *no entry*, with no recipe link and **nothing invented** — it exists nowhere else in the cookbook, and a planning sheet is the right place for a side that isn't a recipe. Four more genuine no-cook items sit with it. **Rubs and sauces are one closing strip and nothing more** — *"I don't think the spices and rubs matter for this particular sheet. It's just a planning sheet"* — mapped to **beef / pork / chicken & poultry / seafood / anything**, per his one allowance: *"maybe we do have our spices and rubs that are linked to steak, chicken, pork, maybe all the above."* **The strip carries only general-purpose blends.** Anything tied to a single recipe stays with that recipe and is off the strip — which cost it [§4.1 Darcy's Steak Rub](codys-cookbook.md#41-darcys-steak-rub), [§4.109 Simon & Garfunkel Rub](codys-cookbook.md#4109-simon--garfunkel-rub), [§4.114 Cowboy Java Rub](codys-cookbook.md#4114-cowboy-java-rub), [§4.23 Remoulade Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#423-remoulade-sauce) and [§4.18 Lemon Butter Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#418-lemon-butter-sauce) despite three of those being things he likes, because each is named by or built into a dish already on the sheet. **Scope, stated on the sheet itself so nobody mistakes it for the index:** ✅ written-up recipes only, **no placeholders** (there is no method to cook yet), **§4 excluded** from both columns, **§14 Desserts excluded** with a closing line saying where they live and offering to add a column. Where a source gave several versions of one dish — four crawfish bisques, six waterfowl gumbos, three courtbouillons, five briskets — **the sheet takes one or two and points at the [Table of Contents](codys-cookbook.md#table-of-contents) for the rest**, which is the difference between a page you can read on a fridge and a reprint of the index. **❤️ and 🥑 carry across** from the [Family Ratings Index](codys-cookbook.md#family-ratings-index) and the keto tagging; 👍 and 🔖 deliberately do not, to keep the page a working sheet rather than another index. **[§7.119](codys-cookbook.md#7119-big-bob-gibsons-chicken-in-bama-white-sauce) needed no placeholder** — Cody thought it might be missing and it has been complete since the Meathead transcription. **One genuinely ambiguous call, recorded rather than buried:** [§5.17 Asparagus on Pasta with a Poached Egg & Lemon Butter](codys-cookbook.md#517-asparagus-on-pasta-with-a-poached-egg--lemon-butter) is filed in §5 Vegetables & Sides but its own entry says *serves 2* and describes a lunch built around fresh pasta — so on this sheet it is a **main**, marked 🍽️. All 9,485 anchor links in the cookbook were re-checked after the insert; the checker was verified against two deliberately broken anchors before the run was trusted. |
| 2026-09-08 | **New [§11.8 Fried Cabbage and Bacon](codys-cookbook.md#118-fried-cabbage-and-bacon)** — transcribed from the keto cookbook's ***Side Dishes*** chapter, the same book as §11.3–§11.7, §10.4 and §7.21. Four ingredients, two steps: bacon crisped and **lifted out with a slotted spoon so every drop of fat stays in the pan**, cabbage cooked in those drippings about 10 min to tender, bacon returned with the salt and pepper for 5 minutes more. **🥑 Keto · 🟢 Easy**, and at a 20-minute total it is **the fastest keto recipe in the cookbook**. **The interesting find is a pattern, not a recipe.** Set beside [§11.7 Home-Fried Bacon Radishes](codys-cookbook.md#117-home-fried-bacon-radishes), this book turns out to have a **house formula for vegetable sides** — crisp chopped bacon, pull it and leave the fat, cook the vegetable in the drippings, return the bacon at the end. Neither recipe lists any other cooking fat because **the bacon is the fat**, which is also why both are keto without substituting anything. That's written up on both entries and in the keto callout, since the pattern transfers to most sturdy vegetables and is more useful than either recipe alone. **Nutrition is the publisher's per §3b.** At **5 g net carbs it is the highest of the eight keto recipes** — and the callout now says why that isn't a knock: cabbage brings real carbohydrate, but it also brings **3.9 g of fiber per serving, the most in the keto set**, which is precisely what pulls the net figure down. The callout is re-sorted to eight recipes and now notes that the two entries substituting *nothing* bracket the whole range — §10.4 lowest because eggs and sausage need no stand-in, §11.8 highest because a vegetable cooked as itself simply carries carbohydrate. **Two source details kept as instructions rather than gloss:** the slotted spoon (tip the bacon into a strainer and you've poured the dish away) and *serve immediately*. **Seasoning goes in at the end** with a note on why — salt draws water out of cabbage and would steam the pan if added at the start. **Flagged rather than guessed:** the page number is outside the photo frame, "1 medium head" is the only cabbage quantity given and no weight was invented, the bacon thickness is unspecified though the rendered fat is the entire cooking medium, and the small **"30" icon** on the page is read as a *30 minutes or less* marker — an inference from the 20-minute total, noted as such rather than asserted. |
| 2026-09-08 | **[§10.4 Southern Keto Egg and Sausage Muffins](codys-cookbook.md#104-southern-keto-egg-and-sausage-muffins) is closed — the longest-standing bare placeholder in the book is now a full recipe.** Cody photographed the page hours after saying he'd go to the keto cookbook for it, and the lead recorded on the placeholder turned out to be exactly right: it is the same book as §11.3–§11.7 and §7.21, in the ***Breakfast & Breads*** chapter, where it's printed simply as **"Egg Muffins."** His longer name stays as the entry title with the source's printed title recorded. Eight ingredients as printed, five steps: sausage browned **with the chives** about 10 min and drained, eggs whisked alone before the cream and seasonings go in, sausage and cheddar stirred through, **10 wells of a 12-well pan filled three-quarters full**, baked 15–20 min at 350°F to a set center and lightly browned top. **The source's own reheating warning is kept as its own step** — a week in the fridge, and reheat just until warm or the eggs go rubbery. **🥑 Keto · 🟢 Easy**, times as printed. **Nutrition is the publisher's per §3b — and at 0.6 g net carbs per muffin it is the lowest figure in the entire cookbook**, which propagated: the keto callout is re-sorted lowest-first and now lists 7 recipes, and **[§11.7 Home-Fried Bacon Radishes](codys-cookbook.md#117-home-fried-bacon-radishes)' claim to be the lowest — written earlier the same day — was corrected in place** to second lowest. The callout also now explains *why* §10.4 wins: **it substitutes nothing.** Eggs, sausage, cream and cheese are low-carb already, so unlike every other keto entry here there's no flour stand-in to cost anything. Also propagated: TOC flipped ○ → ✅ 🥑, a Difficulty & Time Index row added, §10.4 removed from the placeholder list, and §11.7's note about the still-missing chapter rewritten as resolved. His new rating is recorded alongside the old one — ***"our family likes these, it's a solid breakfast"*** beside the placeholder's *"these rock."* **Flagged rather than guessed:** the page number is outside the photo frame, the calorie figure was clipped at the edge and is transcribed as 165 with the arithmetic that supports it, and the sausage is unspecified by brand, heat, or fat content. |
| 2026-09-08 | **New [§11.7 Home-Fried Bacon Radishes](codys-cookbook.md#117-home-fried-bacon-radishes)** — transcribed from Cody's photograph of the keto cookbook, **p. 68, *Breakfast & Breads*** — the same book as §11.3–§11.6 and §7.21. Radishes chopped small, bacon crisped and pulled with **all the drippings left in the pan**, then radishes fried 10 minutes over medium-high and 10 more over medium with the bacon back in, stirring every couple of minutes to caramelized edges. **🥑 Keto · 🟢 Easy**, prep and cook times the source's own. **Nutrition is the publisher's, used as printed per §3b** — 100 cal, 6.9 g fat, 6 g protein, 3.9 g carbs, 1.8 g fiber per serving, whole-dish figures calculated from those at its stated 4 servings. **Net carbs 2 g — the lowest of any recipe in the book**, which reordered the keto callout to run lowest-first. **The substitution pattern in §3c gained a member and a distinction worth keeping:** this is the **third potato swap** in §11, but the first with a vegetable other than cauliflower — [§11.4](codys-cookbook.md#114-southern-fauxtato-salad-cauliflower-potato-salad) and [§11.5](codys-cookbook.md#115-easy-caulimash-keto-mashed-potatoes) use cauliflower for a cold salad and a mash, while **radishes hold their shape and edges in a hot pan**, which is what lets these read as home fries. The callout now says so rather than lumping all three together. **Also flagged: this page is from the exact chapter [§10.4 Southern Keto Egg and Sausage Muffins](codys-cookbook.md#104-southern-keto-egg-and-sausage-muffins) is still waiting on**, so a note on §11.7 points there. **One cookbook gloss, labeled as such:** why a cooked radish reads like a potato — its raw peppery bite comes from volatile mustard-family compounds that heat drives off. Open questions kept rather than invented: the source gives no skillet size beyond "large" and no bacon thickness, both of which move the browning and the numbers. |

The list was right — both were recosted earlier the same day when the butt size closed at bone-in 7–10 lb, and the step didn't follow. Nothing needed adding. |
| 2026-09-08 | **Cody's two brisket methods written up from his own dictation — the most detailed method he has given for any dish — plus the homemade salt one of them runs on, and one deletion he asked for by name.** **[§6.1 Smoked Brisket](codys-cookbook.md#61-smoked-brisket) is no longer a placeholder.** His **straight smoke**: a heavy **overnight dry brine with plain salt**, then **coarse ground black pepper** the next day — *"it has never let me down"* — the pit brought to **225°F and left to settle a full hour**, **a handful of pecan**, and **the meat does not go on until the smoke turns thin and blue**, because the bitter smoke burns off first. Probe to **165°F**, let it stall, then **wrap in foil — or don't wrap at all, the Meathead way, for a better bark**; both are his and the entry presents them as a real choice with the tradeoff stated rather than one method with a footnote. Finished at **203°F**, his standing number across everything he smokes. He says outright the method is basically Meathead's, with **two departures: he doesn't like the Big Bad Beef Rub, and he runs a heavier dry brine** (consistent with [§T37](codys-cookbook.md#t37-salt-brining-and-the-dry-brine--codys-standing-practice-heavy-every-time)). **The sharpest thing in it is his salted-rub warning, and it leads the entry:** if a rub already carries salt, **do not also dry brine with salt** — it comes out too salty *and* most of that second dose never penetrates; **a salted rub goes on the night before**, and **if you dry brined with plain salt, whatever follows must be unsalted.** Written as a two-path table, with this cookbook's own rubs sorted into the two paths. **He also names the method's one weakness himself — with a straight smoke he has no control over when it gets done** — which is why the second entry exists. **NEW — [§6.10 Sous Vide Brisket (Richie's Method)](codys-cookbook.md#610-sous-vide-brisket-richies-method), credited to Richie, Cody's friend and the founder of Q39**, who says it tastes pretty much the same as his championship brisket. **Heavy homemade smoked salt** — go heavier than feels right, because a lot of the salt cooks out over two days — **plus a whole bunch of pepper; a Dalmatian rub, kept simple.** The **point is separated from the flat** so the pieces fit the bags, anything still too big cut **as large as will fit and across the grain**; **butter in the bag**; **vacuum as hard as the sealer will pull**, because the bag loosens as juice releases. **150°F for two days**, then **the whole bag into an ice chest of ice overnight**, then **225°F for about four hours** over a palmful of **pecan** (hickory does great too) for a semi-decent bark, then **the Egg roaring hot for a hot browning — explicitly not a sear** — turned about a minute at a time for **"a nice, salty, smoky chewy bark."** **The sous vide juice is saved, warmed in a pan, and spooned over the slices as you cut.** The tradeoff is stated as he stated it: **it sacrifices some bark and buys juiciness and control — *"you never mess it up"*** — and the smoke comes from **two places**, the four-hour fire and the smoked salt that sat sealed against the meat for two days. **Cross-linked to [§3 Darcy's Steak](codys-cookbook.md#3-darcys-steak)**, whose ice cooler does the same job, **with [§6.9's](codys-cookbook.md#69-codys-pulled-pork) no-ice heat-holding cooler kept distinct from both.** **NEW inside §6.10 — his burnt ends**, as a lettered subsection rather than a separate recipe: made from **the point after the four-hour smoke**, cubed, drizzled with **avocado oil** — a light greasy layer, not heavy — and **pan seared just to crisp the outside, not cooked through, because it's already cooked**; served on a board with barbecue sauce on the side or over half. **Where it came from is recorded:** [§T71](codys-cookbook.md#t71-burnt-ends) notes Meathead frying burnt ends in duck fat would disqualify you in competition, and Cody's answer is *"I'm not in a competition. I'm in it for taste"* — so this is Richie's sous vide plus the fried-burnt-end idea, with avocado oil for the duck fat. **Both briskets are rated 🟢 Easy on [CLAUDE.md §3a](CLAUDE.md)'s own test** — nothing in either can go from perfect to ruined; the elapsed time (12–14 hr of smoke; two days of bath plus four hours of fire) is **Slow cook**, never Cook, and never inflates a rating. §6.10's browning is called out as the one attended step and explained as **lower-risk than [§3](codys-cookbook.md#3-darcys-steak)'s 🟡 chimney sear, because a brisket two days at 150°F has no doneness left to lose.** **NEW — [§4.195 Cody's Smoked Salt](codys-cookbook.md#4195-codys-smoked-salt)**, undocumented until now and load-bearing in §6.10: **coarse sea salt he smokes himself for about a couple of hours; he smokes pink Himalayan too.** **That is all he gave, and the entry says so** — **the 225°F, the indirect setup and the thin-layer instruction are labeled cookbook judgment per [CLAUDE.md §3d](CLAUDE.md)**, reasoned from [§4.115 Smoked Garlic Powder](codys-cookbook.md#4115-smoked-garlic-powder-or-smoked-onion-powder) (Meathead's own method for smoking a dry seasoning at 225°F for 1–2 hours, which is exactly where Cody's two hours falls), with **the wood left an Open Question** because he never named one. Related to, rather than duplicating, the **store-bought hickory-smoked salt** [§4.26 Basic Barbecue Rub](codys-cookbook.md#426-basic-barbecue-rub) already calls for; that entry now points at his. **DELETED at Cody's explicit request — §4.106 Big Bad Beef Rub.** In his words: *"I really don't like the Big Bad Beef Rub that Meathead has. The chemistry behind it is credited to *Cook's Illustrated / America's Test Kitchen, "Keeping the Kick in Fresh Horseradish"*: grating sets off the enzyme reaction that makes the heat, and vinegar slows it, which is exactly what a jar is. [§4.57](codys-cookbook.md#457-fresh-horseradish-butter) is cross-linked for handling the root. ⚙️ **Its proportions are now researched judgment rather than a guess** — *"if there's something on there you can say about these proportions, just guess"* — set from [§4.40](codys-cookbook.md#440-herbed-horseradish-sauce)'s 2–3 Tbsp of prepared horseradish per 1½ cups of cold dairy, scaled up because there's no whipped cream here to dilute it. 🗣️ **Cody's *"it's really a copycat of Outback's, that fresh dill, sour cream, cream cheese kind of thing"* is recorded as his description of the target, not as a sourced fact** — no copycat page was consulted and none will be, per §3d. **His dad's real proportions stay flagged as the ones that matter.** **[§7.19 Honey Chicken Marinade & Kabobs](codys-cookbook.md#719-honey-chicken-marinade--kabobs) — the duplicated onions line closes, and the whole kebab build is his now.** *"I don't know what the duplicated onions are, so just use your judgment, because you're just quartering onions there"* — one onion line, quartered, and the spreadsheet discrepancy stops being carried. Also warned: don't go under ½ inch, because vegetables shrink and pieces cut too small cook through before they take any color. **The diagnosis is Cody's; the figures are cookbook judgment**, reasoned from *Salt, Fat, Acid, Heat* — which does speak to this, as he suspected, making 400°F the default and tying the right temperature to the size and density of the cut — from **America's Test Kitchen's "How to Roast Any Vegetable"** for equal-size pieces and the practical band, and from this book's own [§T47](codys-cookbook.md#t47-cooking-vegetables-and-fruits--and-reverse-searing-them-too) (Meathead: smaller disks and chunks, more surface for browning, err toward undercooking). **In every one of these, his own figures supersede the cookbook's the day he gives them.** |

His wording is quoted in the rule: *real chefs with real credentials, tried-and-true sources, not a Pinterest or Facebook flare.* **Source hierarchy, in order:** this cookbook first (already sourced, and it keeps the book self-consistent — [§T105](codys-cookbook.md#t105-chiles-chipotles-in-adobo-and-paprika-the-meathead-method)'s Scoville table answering the chile question is the model); then named authors already in this book; then professional test kitchens and credentialed chefs; then USDA FSIS for anything touching temperature or safety. **Explicitly barred:** Pinterest and Facebook reposts, SEO recipe farms, and **user-submitted recipe archives and anonymous copycat pages** — the last of those is named because one was used for [§4.7 Captain Mike's](codys-cookbook.md#47-captain-mikes-seasoning) earlier the same day and Cody rejected the result outright. **Write-up requirements, every time:** label the fill as cookbook judgment where a reader will see it; name the source by publication and author rather than attaching a URL that can't be verified from this environment; say which parts are Cody's and which are the cookbook's; state that his own figures supersede; and log it here. **And the limit is stated too — judgment fills gaps, it does not invent facts.** Where no credible source covers it, the item stays an Open Question rather than being dressed up as research. |

Amounts re-cut to satisfy all three of his facts at once: quantities still run downhill in his exact order; **white pepper rises to ~21% of the batch (5 tsp of 24), five times any other non-salt ingredient**; and **the pale three reach 75%**, with **cayenne pulled down to about 4%** (1 tsp, from 2), since very little cayenne turns a white powder pink. Black pepper and the four herbs drop to 1 tsp each. Knock-on corrections: the salt fraction rose to a little over half, so the **usage rate drops to ½ tsp per pound** (from ¾) and the **sodium estimate rises to ~875 mg per teaspoon** (from ~650); the Nutrition block, the grocery list, and the bay-leaf count (4–5 leaves, from 6–8) follow. Step 3 gains a **look test** — the jar should read off-white with flecks, and a pink or tan cast means too much cayenne. **New open question:** whether 1 tsp of cayenne is enough heat for him, since that is the one ingredient where appearance and flavor pull against each other. |
| 2026-09-08 | **[§7.13 Crawfish Elegante](codys-cookbook.md#713-crawfish-elegante) — the re-posed sauce question came back answered the same day, and it closes.** Cody confirmed the sauce **does** simmer after the cream goes in, that **you keep stirring constantly**, that the simmer is there **to thicken it if it needs thickening**, and that **you add flour as needed** to get it there — in his words, not *"a teeny bit of flour,"* just flour, as much as it takes. That became a **new step 5**, pushing the old steps 5–7 down to 6–8. **He gave no number of minutes, and that turns out to be the answer rather than a gap** — it's a thicken-until-it-looks-right step, so step 5 carries the visual cue (coats a spoon, holds a finger line) instead of a clock. The 1½ Tbsp of flour in step 3 is now labeled a starting amount in the ingredients, the instructions, and the grocery list. **How to work that extra flour in without lumps is marked as cookbook gloss, not from the card** — a beurre manié beaten in a little at a time, cross-linked to [§4.70 Sauce Bâtarde](codys-cookbook.md#470-sauce-bâtarde--sauce-au-beurre-mock-hollandaise) and [§7.56 Coq au Vin](codys-cookbook.md#756-coq-au-vin-chicken-in-red-wine-with-onions-mushrooms-and-bacon), which use the same thickener. **The badge stays 🟡** and step 5 says why: a butter-and-flour thickener that never browns is the blond end of the family and earns nothing on its own, and the scorch risk in a cream sauce is a slow one you can see and smell coming, not a dark roux's under-a-minute window. The daughter's validation callout was corrected — she cooked this **before** step 5 was written down, so the step describes what the working method was already doing rather than changing it. **One new question raised rather than quietly resolved: half & half or heavy cream?** Cody described simmering *"the heavy cream,"* but the card says **1 pint of half & half**, and the entry still says so. They aren't interchangeable here — heavy cream is roughly 36% fat against half & half's 10–12%, so it thickens faster, resists breaking, and would need less of step 5's added flour. Flagged in the ingredients, the grocery list, and Open Questions. |

Written into steps 10 and 11. **The chuck-slicing question stays open** — he was asked again and simply doesn't know, so the paper-thin instruction stands and the question is recorded as asked-and-unanswered rather than deleted. **[§4.7 Captain Mike's Seasoning](codys-cookbook.md#47-captain-mikes-seasoning) — the single most useful fact anyone has given this entry: the nine ingredients were listed in *descending order of quantity*, most first and least last**, and **white pepper is prominent and tasteable**. That turns a bare list into a structure — the two salts lead, white pepper is third and so is the largest non-salt ingredient in the jar. The proportions were re-cut to obey it (5 tsp / 4½ / 4 / 2 / 2 / 2 / 1½ / 1½ / 1½ in a ½-cup batch, white pepper ~17% against the 4–10% of ordinary Creole mixes), still labeled **cookbook judgment**, reasoned from Emeril's Essence, the Seafood Magic copycat, and this book's own Prudhomme mixes **— ⛔ superseded the same day; Cody rejected all of that basis, see the newest 2026-09-08 row —** at [§4.16](codys-cookbook.md#416-new-orleans-italian-red-gravy)/[§4.17](codys-cookbook.md#417-giblet-gravy). The list must never be re-sorted — the order *is* the data. **And the search for a written original ends: it isn't on a card anywhere**, so that stops being a pending item. **[§7.13 Crawfish Elegante](codys-cookbook.md#713-crawfish-elegante) — not closed, on purpose, but it gained the strongest evidence any entry in this book has: Cody's daughter cooked it off the method as written here and it came out perfectly.** He doesn't know the answer to the sauce-thickening question himself and said to ask him again, so the question was **re-posed precisely** — does the sauce need a simmer after the half & half goes in, and if so how many minutes, at what heat, and what's the visual cue — rather than marked settled. **[§7.14 Chicken Oscar](codys-cookbook.md#714-chicken-oscar-court-of-2-sisters) closed as judgment**: the garlic in oil and green onion go into the deglazed pan for ~30 seconds, immediately before the crabmeat, which warms 1–2 minutes — reasoned from Prudhomme's butter-sauce sequence at [§4.8](codys-cookbook.md#48-shrimp-and-crab-butter-cream-sauce) and this book's own [§7.13](codys-cookbook.md#713-crawfish-elegante). **[§5.37 Mamma's Spinach](codys-cookbook.md#537-mammas-spinach): Mamma is Cody's mom** — recorded, and the missing salt quantity turns out not to be a gap but the method. **The trick is the lemon-and-salt ratio**, acid and salt in balance, found by tasting; step 4 now teaches how to find it (sour or thin → more salt; flat or dull → more lemon). A presumed-same-Mamma note was added to [§8.33](codys-cookbook.md#833-mammas-deerbeef-stew) as a question rather than an assertion, since he was only asked about §5.37. **[§7.19 Honey Chicken Marinade & Kabobs](codys-cookbook.md#719-honey-chicken-marinade--kabobs) — the onion method, which is a real technique and not a loading order:** quarter the onions, thread them, hit them **generously with Greek seasoning (Cavender's)**, and **set them right against the kielbasa so the sausage juice runs into the onion.** Kielbasa gives rather than absorbs on the fire, and the onion is what catches it. **The duplicated "onions" line on the sheet stays open** — he needs to look. **[§7.59 Mediterranean Pork Tenderloin Platter](codys-cookbook.md#759-mediterranean-pork-tenderloin-platter-vegeta-rubbed-sous-vide--seared) — feta: "absolutely not."** He prefers **goat cheese, crumbled**, which closes the long-standing feta wish carried at [§9.21](codys-cookbook.md#921-dressed-up-hummus-with-garlic-naan) against and makes no-feta the house position across the Mediterranean dishes, matching [§5.23](codys-cookbook.md#523-greek-salad-with-crumbled-goat-cheese). Plus a practical note of his: since this platter's cheese is *spread* rather than crumbled, **ordinary spreadable goat cheese may be both better and cheaper** than buying logs and beating them soft — propagated through the ingredients, step 8, step 10, and the grocery list, with crumbled goat cheese added as an optional finish. **[§8.1 Chili Mac](codys-cookbook.md#81-chili-mac) — an important correction: the chili goes in the crock pot, not the chili mac as a whole.** The noodles are never in the slow cooker; they're boiled separately and the chili goes over them. Worth being blunt about, because "crock-pot chili mac" recipes that dump dry pasta into the pot are common and this is not one. Fixed in the header, the badge, the ingredients, steps 2–3, the grocery list, and both index references. **Crock-pot time set as judgment — 4 hr on low** (floor 2, ceiling 8), shortened from the 6–8 hr of Southern Living's and Betty Crocker's slow-cooker chilis because this pot goes in with the meat already browned and canned chili already cooked: it's a meld, not a cook. **[§8.3 Chicken and Andouille Gumbo](codys-cookbook.md#83-chicken-and-andouille-smoked-sausage-gumbo): the 45-minute simmer stands**, an hour is the ceiling — judgment, reasoned from the source page (Prudhomme) read against this book's other eight gumbos, whose simmer times scale with how tough the meat is and nothing else. Nothing in this pot is tough, so more time only reduces; when you want more flavor, take the roux darker instead. **[§8.4 Lemon Chicken Soup](codys-cookbook.md#84-lemon-chicken-soup-with-spinach-and-rice) — both halves of one answer: the rice *does* hold up as leftovers**, so no cooking it separately; **but you can put too much rice in.** It keeps expanding in the soup and the whole pot goes rice-heavy. ⅓ cup is now flagged as a hard number in the ingredients, at step 2, and on the grocery list, with a new step 8 on leftovers. **[§8.30 Turtle Soup I](codys-cookbook.md#830-turtle-soup-i): Martha Paggi is the author**, correcting the earlier reading that took her name for a running header. **The printed recipe is also signed Dr. J. J. Burdin** — both names are recorded, and **how they relate is left explicitly unresolved** rather than explained away. **[§T17 The Raw Egg Soak](codys-cookbook.md#t17-the-raw-egg-soak-deodorizing-fish--shellfish): cold water rinse only — confirmed**, no longer an assumption. **[§10.4 Southern Keto Egg and Sausage Muffins](codys-cookbook.md#104-southern-keto-egg-and-sausage-muffins) stays a placeholder.** Cody believed he had already given this recipe; a repo-wide search — the section number, the name, every alternate wording, every "muffin" and every "Southern Keto" mention, and the changelog history — **found no content for it anywhere**. He said he'll clarify and supply it himself, so nothing was invented; a lead is noted, that the same printed keto cookbook behind [§11.3](codys-cookbook.md#113-keto-skillet-cornbread)–[§11.6](codys-cookbook.md#116-keto-zucchini-bread) has the *Breakfast & Breads* chapter where it would live. |
| 2026-09-08 | **Two placeholders and thin cards became real write-ups from Cody's own dictation — [§3 Darcy's Steak](codys-cookbook.md#3-darcys-steak) and [§5.38 Roasted Veggies](codys-cookbook.md#538-roasted-veggies).** **§3 is no longer a placeholder.** Cody's steak method is written up in full, and the thing to lead with is *what the sous vide is for*: **he uses it to make a cheap, lower-quality cut tender**, not to cook a prime steak. **About 6 hours for a cheap cut, 4 hours as a general minimum**; **128–129°F for thicker steaks, 125°F for thinner ones held a long time**. **Patting dry is the single most important step** — he keeps a **dedicated black towel bought only for patting sous vide meat**, and the entry explains why (a wet surface steams instead of searing). **Avocado oil spray** after patting, for its high smoke point, where olive oil would burn. The sear happens **over a roaring charcoal chimney with a small grate on top** — insta-light briquettes or lump — and the entry explains what a chimney sear is and why the updraft column runs far hotter than a grill grate, cross-linked to [§7.134 Afterburner Fajitas](codys-cookbook.md#7134-afterburner-fajitas) and [§7.107 Skinny Steaks](codys-cookbook.md#7107-skinny-steaks), which already document it. **The ice cooler gets its own numbered step** (Step 4): the bagged steak goes into a **cooler packed with ice** and chills hard, then it is patted dry and oiled **while still cold**, so the sear can run **longer without cooking through** — a cold interior buys seconds of surface contact before the center climbs. **⚠️ And the two coolers in this cookbook are now impossible to confuse, which Cody asked for explicitly.** A prominent callout in §3 and a matching one in [§6.9 Cody's Pulled Pork](codys-cookbook.md#69-codys-pulled-pork)'s Step 6 say it in both directions: **§6.9's cooler has NO ice** — it is an insulated box holding heat in, keeping the butt hot and steaming — while **§3's cooler is packed with ICE**, chilling a bagged steak down. Same equipment, opposite job; cross-linked each way. Seasoning was already documented, so §3 points at [§4.1 Darcy's Steak Rub](codys-cookbook.md#41-darcys-steak-rub) (salt and pepper first, adjusted for thickness, rub layered on top) rather than repeating it, and [§1 Cody's Pho](codys-cookbook.md#1-codys-pho)'s anaerobic-bag rule — **dry rub only, no fresh garlic, no liquid** — is cross-referenced rather than restated. **Rated 🟡 Medium** per [CLAUDE.md §3a](CLAUDE.md): the bath cannot go from perfect to ruined and its hours are **Slow cook**, but the chimney sear can — a steak already at its finished temperature over a 1,000°F-plus fire has no doneness cushion left. Same call [§T27](codys-cookbook.md#t27-sous-vide-not-so-premium-steak-cuts-codys-method) makes for the cast-iron version, and §T27's *Used in* line now names §3. **🚫 The chimney brand is deliberately left unidentified** — Cody was unsure of it and what he said sounded like *"tomato gels"* or *"trader gels"*; **no brand was guessed at or substituted**, and it is recorded as an open question along with the cut and thickness, how long "a long time" at 125°F is, and the sear duration in seconds. Placeholder banner removed; TOC flipped ○ → ✅; a [Difficulty & Time Index](codys-cookbook.md#difficulty--time-index) row added (15 min prep / 20 min cook / 4–6 hr slow cook); the §12 status row and the two remaining "§3 is a placeholder" lines corrected. **§5.38 keeps its "wing it" character but now carries his actual practice.** **An elevated rack or grid goes on the sheet pan so air gets underneath** — the difference between roasting and steaming, tied into [§T39](codys-cookbook.md#t39-maillard-vs-caramelization--gbd)'s existing point about giving vegetables room to brown. **Olive oil drizzled, rosemary sprigs, then season.** **400°F is now sourced rather than guessed** — it is ***Salt, Fat, Acid, Heat***'s number, the same source as [§T29](codys-cookbook.md#t29-how-to-salt)–[§T32](codys-cookbook.md#t32-how-to-use-heat), so the card's old hedge that no oven temperature was given is gone. **His vegetables come out firm because he pulls them before they burn**, written as the honest tradeoff it is. **His own unresolved question — whether 375°F would give a slightly softer vegetable — is recorded as a question and a thing to try, not as an answer**; the recipe stays at 400°F. Provenance is stated line by line in the entry: card, Cody, or SFAH. Badge and index row unchanged — the timing did not move. |
| 2026-09-08 | **Cody dictated answers to a run of long-standing open questions, and they close across four entries.** **[§6.9 Cody's Pulled Pork](codys-cookbook.md#69-codys-pulled-pork) — five of its six open questions are now answered.** **The butt is bone-in, 7 to 10 lb** *("seven to eleven pounds sometimes… I don't know, seven to ten pounds, something like that")* — the last unlabeled assumption in the entry is gone, and the yield, salt and rub quantities, grocery amounts and the whole Nutrition block are **recosted across that range with both ends shown** instead of around a guessed 8 lb butt *(one butt now serves 10–14; a four-or-five-butt load is 28–50 lb raw, 20–35 lb pulled, serving 40–70)*. **The wood is pecan — hickory works, but pecan is his favorite** — written into Step 4 as the preference with hickory as the standard alternative, which also squares this entry with the hickory in [§7.9](codys-cookbook.md#79-caveman-chicken-wings). **The cooler rest is 1 to 3 hours and he thinks it's great**, so Step 6 now says plainly that the rest is *part of the method, not a compromise*, and the badge, the index row and the headnote all carry 1–3 hr instead of "multi-hour." **The white vinegar goes in to taste** — a little, taste, repeat — and **its job is to balance the richness of the pork**, which is the half of the answer that tells you when to stop; it is no longer carried as an unquantified open item. **He does not trim the fat cap at all**, so [§T40](codys-cookbook.md#t40-the-fat-cap--trim-or-not)'s ¼–⅛ inch stays as the book's general guidance and not as his practice. **Left open on purpose: the water pan** — he moved past it without answering, and that bullet is untouched. Also recorded because he said it outright while reconciling it against the wings: **there is no baking powder anywhere in the pulled pork** — a one-line note under Step 3 pointing at [§T96](codys-cookbook.md#t96-coating-the-chicken--dredges-batters-and-why-baking-powder-works-the-meathead-method) for why the wings are a different problem. **NEW — Cody's smoke-wood rule, added to [§T35 Smoke Science](codys-cookbook.md#t35-smoke-science--combustion-the-smoke-ring-and-getting-blue-smoke) as its own closing subsection, *Cody's rule — don't overload the pit with wood*.** He called it *"the tip of the day"* and it's a technique, not a pork detail: **do not put too much raw wood in the pit — only a couple of chunks, about a handful, the size that fits in the palm of your hand.** More than that and it genuinely tastes too smoky — *"I used to not think that was a possibility, but it is."* The subsection is **labeled as Cody's own practice rather than the book's**, and it's placed against Meathead's own figures, where a palmful (~4–8 oz) sits at the **bottom** of the 4–16 oz range even for a twelve-hour butt. **Cross-linked from every entry of his that involves smoke:** [§6.9](codys-cookbook.md#69-codys-pulled-pork) *(Step 4 and the grocery list)*, [§7.9](codys-cookbook.md#79-caveman-chicken-wings), and the four placeholders [§6.1](codys-cookbook.md#61-smoked-brisket), [§6.3](codys-cookbook.md#63-smoked-whole-hen), [§6.4](codys-cookbook.md#64-smoked-steak) and [§6.5](codys-cookbook.md#65-smoked-ribs), plus [§T22 Rib Marinade](codys-cookbook.md#t22-rib-marinade--dry-brine-or-wet-marinade-codys-method) — so each placeholder's open "wood choice" question is now **half-answered before it's written**. **The consistency is worth naming:** [§7.9](codys-cookbook.md#79-caveman-chicken-wings) has said *"one hickory chunk. One."* since it was written, so the rule was already implicit in his practice and is now stated once, centrally. **[§7.9 Caveman Chicken Wings](codys-cookbook.md#79-caveman-chicken-wings) — the baking-powder question closes, and the method is rewritten around the answer.** The powder goes on **before the oil and before the air-dry**: *"I salt the wings — or Tony's the wings — and then put some baking powder on them, and then that'll get into the wings."* So the sequence is now **Tony's + baking powder together → the overnight uncovered air-dry → the oil spray the next day, just before they go on**. That is the conventional order, and it **resolves the A/B in favor of the alternative the entry had been carrying only as a 💡 "worth trying" suggestion** — Step 1 and Step 3 are rewritten, the ingredient list says which items go on which day, and the headnote, TOC line and ingredient index all follow. **His reasoning is kept because it's the part that generalizes** — *that'll get into the wings*: baking powder works by hours of contact with the skin, not by a dusting onto oil minutes before the heat. **The old order is preserved as a note, not deleted** — oil-then-powder on the morning of the cook is what he did the first time, it worked, and it's recorded as a fallback with the reason it changed. **Reframed rather than closed: the sauce question.** He doesn't want the Buffalo Wild Wings default settled; that Open Question is rewritten as a **research request — find a healthier bottled wing sauce with no corn syrup and no vegetable oil, ideally stocked at Walmart.** **[§9.6 Beef Jerky](codys-cookbook.md#96-beef-jerky) and [§9.29 Deer Jerky](codys-cookbook.md#929-deer-jerky) — the long-standing timing ambiguity is RESOLVED, and it changes the plan for the day.** *"1.5 to 1.75 hours/tray swap"* on the card is a **rotation interval, not the total dry time**: *"one and a half hour, one point seven-five hour tray swap — that means you go from the bottom tray to the top tray, and what it does is it just keeps the dehydrating process even. If you've got a new dehydrator, that may not even be necessary."* So **every 1½–1¾ hours the trays move bottom-to-top**, and **the dry ends on the bend test**, which both entries already said — in practice **3 to 6 hours** for ¼-inch ground strips. That also reconciles the card with its own crossed-out *150° for 8–10 hr*, which had looked like a contradiction. **Corrected identically in both entries, since they're one recipe:** Step 4's text, the ⚠️ ambiguity callout *(now a resolved note)*, the Open Question, the badge *(**Slow cook ~3–6 hr**, start to finish **~11–31 hr**, replacing ~2 hr and 10–26 hr)*, and both **Difficulty & Time Index** rows. **His aside is recorded too** — a newer dehydrator with even airflow may not need the swap at all, worth testing once. **One inference withdrawn:** §9.6's ⚠️ had reasoned from a "1¾-hour dry time" that the machine must run hot; with the number reread as a swap interval, that no longer follows and the note says so. |
