| 2026-09-08 | **Cody dictated answers to the open questions on two of his own recipes, after cooking both — [§7.9 Caveman Chicken Wings](codys-cookbook.md#79-caveman-chicken-wings) and [§6.9 Cody's Pulled Pork](codys-cookbook.md#69-codys-pulled-pork).** **[§7.9](codys-cookbook.md#79-caveman-chicken-wings) — the name finally means something, and it changes the recipe.** *"Remember these are big wings, it's where the drumstick is attached to the wing itself — they're called caveman wings."* A **caveman wing is the whole, uncut wing with the drumette still attached** — not party-cut. That **closes the party-cut Open Question**, which the entry had been carrying as a labeled cookbook assumption, and **the assumption was wrong**: the piece count drops from *"roughly 40 pieces once party-cut"* to **16 to 20 whole wings** at 3–4 oz apiece, the serving sizes are restated in whole wings (**4 to 5 as a meal, 2 as an appetizer**), the grocery line now says **do not have them party-cut**, and Nutrition is recalculated on the new count. The name is also now explained in the headnote — it describes the wing, not the fire — while keeping the existing gloss that *caveman* in barbecue normally means cooking straight on the coals, which this still isn't. **Cook time is Cody's own number now, and the framing is the point.** He cooked them about **1 hr 10 min**, and was explicit about how to write that: *"don't say that — you really have to check them."* So the entry does **not** print a flat 1:10. It says **plan on roughly an hour to an hour and fifteen, start checking at 45 minutes, and pull each wing when its skin is crisp, not when a timer says so** — with the researched **45–50 min** figure kept visible as the *earliest* they might be ready rather than deleted, and the note explaining that the Serious Eats / Food Network / Chili Pepper Madness timings ran short because all three time **party-cut** pieces. **His grill-zone insight is now its own step, in his words:** a grill has hot spots and wings in different zones finish at different times, some much darker than others; **on his Egg the back is hotter, so the back wings come off first**, and you **pull them off one at a time as each one crisps** instead of lifting the whole grate at once. That replaces the old "cook X minutes then remove" phrasing. **A sauce finish is new to the entry:** **bottled Buffalo Wild Wings sauce — Asian Zing and their hot wing sauces, "various kinds"** — added **after** they come off the grill, while hot, by **putting the wings in a plastic Tupperware or lidded container and shaking**, never brushed. His amount, recorded as **his preference and not a rule**, is **about 2 Tbsp per wing** — roughly **2 to 2½ cups, a bottle and a half**, for the batch as written — after he talked himself toward 3 and landed on *"let's go with two, I don't know, use your judgment, remember these are big wings"*; **3 Tbsp is noted as the wetter option.** Added to Ingredients as an optional finish (undressed is still a valid wing), to Grocery (**🛒 Buffalo Wild Wings Asian Zing sauce**, **🛒 Buffalo Wild Wings hot wing sauce**, plus the lidded container), and to **Nutrition, honestly** — the block now carries **unsauced and sauced rows** and says what the sauce actually costs: at 2 Tbsp per wing, Asian Zing adds about **+1,000 calories and +220 g of carbohydrate** to the batch (**+250 cal, +56 g carbs** per meal serving), almost all of it sugar, while a Buffalo-style hot sauce adds roughly half the calories and **almost no carbs** — and either one puts **8,000–10,000 mg of sodium** on the batch, which is the number that actually goes wild. **Badge Cook field 50 min → 1 hr – 1 hr 15 min**, start-to-finish 1 hr 5 min → **1 hr 30 min**, and the [Difficulty & Time Index](codys-cookbook.md#difficulty--time-index) row re-sorted from 65 to **85 min prep + cook**. Table of Contents parenthetical and the [§17](codys-cookbook.md#17-meats--cuts-reference-index) "Chicken wings" line both updated to say *whole, uncut*. **Two Open Questions remain** (the baking-powder order, still awaiting an A/B) **and one is newly raised** — which BWW sauce is the house default, since Asian Zing and a hot sauce are nutritionally nothing alike. The *"let it go for one hour"* ambiguity is marked **mostly moot**: his cook ran about an hour and the Egg still gets its own hour, so both readings are true. **[§6.9](codys-cookbook.md#69-codys-pulled-pork) — the biggest planning gap in the book is closed: it's an overnight cook, and he runs four or five butts at a time.** The entry had carried *"what size butt, and therefore how long does this actually take?"* as its largest open question, with a **cookbook-judgment 12–16 hr** estimate built from an hours-per-pound rule. **His real schedule: on the pit around 10 p.m., off past 10 a.m., sometimes longer — 12 to 14+ hours at 225°F**, and the entry now says plainly that **it is done at temperature, not at a clock time — it comes off at 203°F internal whenever that arrives**, with the cooler rest absorbing the difference. The badge's Slow cook field goes **~12–16 hr → ~12–14+ hr *(225°F overnight; on ~10 p.m., off past 10 a.m.)***, and the [Difficulty & Time Index](codys-cookbook.md#difficulty--time-index) row with it; prep + cook is unchanged at 20 min, so its sort position doesn't move. **The pit is held all night by an Egg Genius**, which is now explained for anyone who doesn't own one — a **fan-driven temperature controller for the Big Green Egg**: a probe reads the pit, a blower on the bottom vent speeds up or slows down, and it holds a set temperature by itself; BBQ Guru, FireBoard and Flame Boss make the same thing, and the category name is a **pit controller**. **Without one you are the controller** — the note says so, and says to expect to babysit it: vents set by hand, a long settle before the meat goes on, a probe alarm, and a check or two in the night. It also notes this is a forgiving enough cook that a drift to 250°F costs nothing but time, which is why the 🟢 stands either way. **Four or five butts at a time is now folded through the whole entry**, not just mentioned: the **yield** (5 lb and serves 10 *per butt*, but **20–25 lb serving 40–50** off a real load), the **ingredient list** (relabeled explicitly *per butt*), **Step 4** (a new passage on running a full pit), **Step 8's vacuum-seal-with-butter freezing**, which is stated as *the reason* for the bulk cook — pulled pork freezes better than almost anything else in this book — the **grocery quantities** (rosemary and butter per butt, a batch and a half of Memphis Dust, the big roll of vacuum bags, and a note that five 8-pounders is 40 lb of raw pork and merits a call to the butcher), and the **Nutrition block**, which now carries per-butt, 4-butt and 5-butt rows with a callout insisting the **per-serving row is the one that matters** — nobody eats 25 lb at a sitting, and the load rows exist for the freezer math. **A full load does not meaningfully change the cook time per butt** — [§T51](codys-cookbook.md#t51-cooking-more-than-one-large-piece-of-meat-at-once)'s finding, cited in Step 4: once the pit recovers, each piece cooks independently in about the time one alone would, since heat enters each butt through its own surface — **provided you keep 2 inches between them**, crowded butts behaving like one big hunk of meat. §T51's *Used in* line now names §6.9 as the cookbook's live case for that entry. **Also new to Step 5:** on a multi-butt load they won't all hit 203°F together, so pull each as it gets there. **Open Questions:** the butt-size question is **half-closed** — the time is answered and the count is answered (four or five), **but he still has not given a per-butt weight**, so the 8 lb figure the entry is costed around remains a labeled assumption; a new closed row records the overnight timing. **The other five are left exactly as they were, because he didn't address them:** the wood for pork, the water pan, how long the cooler rest actually runs, how much white vinegar goes in, and whether he trims the fat cap. Table of Contents parenthetical, the [§17](codys-cookbook.md#17-meats--cuts-reference-index) "Pork shoulder / Boston butt" line, and the five-way pulled pork comparison table's Fire column all updated to say *overnight*. |
| 2026-09-08 | **The second *Louisiana Kitchen* sauce batch, the *Prudhomme Family Cookbook* boudin pages, and *The Barbecue! Bible*'s two peanut sauces — five entries verified, two recipes added, and this project's longest-running mystery solved.** Every page was confirmed by its **printed footer and running head** before anything was transcribed, which mattered more than usual here: **two different Prudhomme books are in this batch** — *Chef Paul Prudhomme's Louisiana Kitchen* (pp. 254, 258–261, 265–266) and ***The Prudhomme Family Cookbook*** (pp. 244–246) — and the running head is the only thing that tells them apart. **🔓 THE §12 MYSTERY SAUCE IS SOLVED, AND IT WAS ALREADY IN THIS COOKBOOK.** Since 2026-08-10, [§12](codys-cookbook.md#12-recipes-to-document) has carried a row for *"the New Orleans blended crab & shrimp sauce Cody loves"* — from *Louisiana Kitchen*, page unknown, and explicitly ruled **not** [§4.8](codys-cookbook.md#48-shrimp-and-crab-butter-cream-sauce) and **not** [§4.21](codys-cookbook.md#421-czarina-sauce-with-shrimp-or-crawfish). **It is [§4.8 Shrimp and Crab Butter Cream Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#48-shrimp-and-crab-butter-cream-sauce), source p. 254.** The photograph settles it: it is the only crab-and-shrimp-together sauce in that chapter, and the source sells it exactly as Cody described it. **Two details of the memory don't survive the page and are recorded rather than smoothed over — there is no blender in the method and there are no oysters in the list** — and the blender is precisely what made three earlier passes rule §4.8 out. The row is closed to §4.8, and the resolution is noted in §4.8, in §12, and in [§13](codys-cookbook.md#13-meathead-cookbook), whose 2026-09-08 note said the batch had moved nothing. **Five entries verified against the printed pages.** **[§4.8](codys-cookbook.md#48-shrimp-and-crab-butter-cream-sauce)** — every quantity correct; one method fix, the **salt and red pepper go in with the cream while the pan is still on high**, before the heat comes down, which this entry had backwards; and its 🟡 is now explained rather than assumed — the roux here is **blond**, one minute and pulled the instant it colors, which per the [roux tiers](codys-cookbook.md#difficulty--time-index) earns no 🔴 at all, so the Medium is the butter-and-cream sauce and the shrimp. **[§4.9 Hollandaise](codys-cookbook.md#49-hollandaise-sauce)** — all seven quantities and every timing correct, including the 6-to-8-minute whip and the ¼-cup / 1-cup / ½-cup / ⅓-cup butterfat-and-solids arithmetic; **the page range was wrong and is fixed to pp. 258–260**, since the whole butterfat finish is on p. 260. It also gains a **raw-yolk safety note, labeled as cookbook judgment** — the page says nothing about egg safety, and these yolks never fully cook. **[§4.10 New Orleans Béarnaise](codys-cookbook.md#410-new-orleans-béarnaise-sauce)** — **its page number is settled at last: p. 261**, read off a clean footer, closing an Open Question that had guessed "260 or so." That also means [§7.3](codys-cookbook.md#73-seafood-stuffed-flounder)'s card citing "page 306" is not this book's pagination. **[§4.11 Choron](codys-cookbook.md#411-choron-sauce)** — same page, quantities correct; one word restored to the doneness cue, the tomatoes cook until **tender** *and* very little liquid remains. **[§4.21 Czarina Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#421-czarina-sauce-with-shrimp-or-crawfish)** — **nothing needed correcting**; all eleven quantities, the 4-Tbsp/1-Tbsp butter split, both headnote portion rules and the julienne NOTE's two **⅛-inch** figures all read clean *(both were re-read on purpose — this project has caught a ⅛ printed as ⅜ before)*. **[§7.51 Boudin Blanc](codys-cookbook.md#751-boudin-blanc-boudin)'s worn step-8 photo is now legible and its reconstruction flag comes off**, along with six corrections: only **2½ tsp of the red pepper** goes in at the mixing stage, not the whole tablespoon *in all*, and the source's cue is **"add more if the red pepper taste isn't clearly present,"* not "if you want more heat"; the lid sits **askew**; runny stuffing takes **1 to 3 cups** more rice **one cup at a time**; the poach **is brought to 180°F over high heat** before dropping to hold 175–185°F, a target the entry didn't have; the boudin is **drained and rested 15 minutes before slicing**; and the make-ahead is now the printed one — **very loosely packed Ziploc bags, an ice-water bath for 1 hour 30 minutes or until the thickest part of a link reads 40°F or less, all surfaces of each bag in contact with the ice water, then refrigerate**. **An earlier version had added freezing, which the page never mentions; that is struck.** **Two recipes added.** **[Fried Boudin Patties](codys-cookbook.md#d-variation--fried-boudin-patties)** (*Prudhomme Family Cookbook* p. 245, its NOTE finishing on p. 246) is filed as **Section D of [§7.51](codys-cookbook.md#751-boudin-blanc-boudin)** rather than as a new number, because its entire ingredient list is *"½ cup leftover chilled stuffing for each patty"* — **and because this cookbook has already ruled on this exact case twice**, folding in [§8.18](codys-cookbook.md#818-gazpacho)'s Gazpacho Cooler *"the same treatment as §7.51's Fried Boudin Patties"* and holding back the companion **Fried Crawfish Patties** rather than let it stand alone without its boudin. It still carries its own badge, Nutrition block, Table of Contents line and [Difficulty & Time Index](codys-cookbook.md#difficulty--time-index) row. Its best line is the source's own: **if the stuffing isn't well chilled it breaks apart and makes a fantastic fried rice** — a failure mode that is simply a different good dish, which is why it rates 🟢. **[§4.190 Thai Peanut Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#4190-thai-peanut-sauce)** and **[§4.191 Dutch West Indian Peanut Sauce (Pindasaus)](codys-cookbook.md#4191-dutch-west-indian-peanut-sauce-pindasaus)** come from the **left and right columns of one two-up page**, *The Barbecue! Bible* p. 476 — read separately at native resolution precisely because column bleed on two-up pages is a mistake this cookbook has caught more than once; nothing crossed over. **§4.190 closes [§7.75 Lettuce Bundles](codys-cookbook.md#775-lettuce-bundles-with-grilled-beef)'s one open component**, the *"Thai Peanut Sauce (page 476)"* it is built around and has cited by page since it was added. **There are now three peanut sauces in this cookbook and all three are different recipes**, so each carries a mutual disambiguation note and a Table of Contents parenthetical, the way the [§4.22](codys-cookbook.md#422-mustard-sauce)/[§4.38](codys-cookbook.md#438-mustard-sauce) "Mustard Sauce" pair was handled: [§4.41](codys-cookbook.md#441-asian-peanut-sauce) is *How to Grill*'s, 2 cups and the only one that starts with a **sauté**; §4.190 is the Thai one, 1 cup, no oil, everything into a cold pan; and **§4.191 is the odd one out — no coconut milk at all**, built on tamarind, ketjap manis and vinegar. **☯️ Ketjap manis is the Hong Kong Market item in §4.191, and one bottle covers two recipes** — [§4.183 Ketjap Butter](codys-cookbook.md#4183-ketjap-butter) is the other, and the two sources give slightly different substitutes, both now recorded in both places. §4.191 also prints **¾ cup of water only inside its Step 1, never in the ingredient list** — listed anyway, the same fix [§4.186](codys-cookbook.md#4186-thai-sweet-chile-sauce-nam-jim-kai) needed. **Three references named but not transcribed, tracked in [§12](codys-cookbook.md#12-recipes-to-document) rather than invented:** *Barbecue! Bible* **p. 241** Tamarind Water (§4.191 prints thawed frozen tamarind puree as its own equal alternate, so nothing is blocked), **p. 73** Dutch West Indian Chicken Kebabs, and **p. 522** Coconut Milk. Table of Contents, Difficulty & Time Index (sorted by prep + cook) and all Nutrition blocks updated. |

Madeira or dry vermouth, a 3-egg-and-1-cup-cream custard with **1 Tbsp tomato paste**, and a 25-to-30-minute bake in the upper third — rated **🟡 Medium** to match end of §10 but shelved beside the other two quiches. **Four entries corrected against the printed pages.** **[§9.20 Pickled Shrimp & Celery](codys-cookbook.md#920-pickled-shrimp--celery)** was missing **½ cup of mirin** entirely, and its brine had picked up **"1 onion, finely chopped"** — a line that belongs to [§8.13 Lobster Stew](codys-cookbook.md#813-lobster-stew) in the facing column of the same page. There is no onion in this recipe. The white wine belongs to the celery pot, not the brine, and the invented second 2 Tbsp of poaching sugar is gone; the open question about the two liquids is answered — the brine is cooked alone and never touches the food until the end, while the **celery pot does double duty and poaches the shrimp in its own leftover liquid**. **[§8.13 Lobster Stew](codys-cookbook.md#813-lobster-stew)**: the shells are **picked out, not strained**; they cook **in butter alone for 10 minutes** with no liquid in the pot; the **cream goes in at the very end with the lobster**, not with the potatoes; and the page citation was off by one — it is **p. 255**, not 254. **[§7.47 Shrimp Roast](codys-cookbook.md#747-shrimp-roast)**: every one of its eighteen quantities is correct, but **turmeric, salt, and the ¼ cup coconut oil had been filed under the spice blend** when the printed *For the shrimp* list simply continues at the top of the second column — the same two-column trap as §9.20. The onions go over **medium** heat, and the tomato paste goes in with the aromatics, not with the spice blend. **[§7.48 Corned Beef & Cabbage](codys-cookbook.md#748-corned-beef--cabbage)** (Canal House p. 338) verified line for line — all fifteen quantities correct — with the **melted butter restored to the method**, where it had been listed as an ingredient and then never used, plus the potatoes' own 10-minute head start. It now carries a **plain-language warning that pink curing salt is sodium nitrite**, dyed pink precisely so it is never confused with kosher salt, and that the two are not interchangeable in either direction. **[§7.11 Corned Beef and Cabbage](codys-cookbook.md#711-corned-beef-and-cabbage) — Cody's own version, from his spreadsheet — was checked against that printed recipe and deliberately left alone**; they are two different dishes and both stand. **[§7.58 Poulet Sauté](codys-cookbook.md#758-poulet-sauté-sautéed-chicken)** had two passages flagged as reconstructions and both are now read clean, along with **seven real corrections**: browning is **8 to 10 minutes**, not 20 to 25 (that's the finish); **the wings cook with the white meat**, not the dark; the deglazing stock is **¾ to 1 cup**; **2 or 3 Tbsp of butter** was missing from the master recipe and **1 tsp of basil** from the Provence variation; that variation's garlic goes in with the **dark** meat and only the **white** meat is removed after browning; and **both (\*) make-ahead notes from p. 256 were missing entirely** and are now transcribed. **Poulet Sauté Chasseur** is added as a named variation that does nothing but point at the veal scallops on book p. 368 — recorded as named-not-transcribed rather than invented. **What is still missing, all newly tracked in [§12](codys-cookbook.md#12-recipes-to-document):** Mastering **p. 258** (the Provence sauce's final buttering — p. 257 stops mid-sentence at *"Remove from heat and beat in"*), **p. 368** (the chasseur method), **p. 483** brown-braised onions and **p. 513** sautéed mushrooms (cited by three recipes between them, the most-referenced untranscribed pages in this cookbook's Mastering material), and Canal House's **Parsley Sauce** on the page facing p. 338. His method as given: **salt it hard the night before** with **Morton kosher salt** — *"I salt that sucker down like crazy… a heavy amount"* — then uncovered on a rack in the fridge overnight, which *is* the dry brine ([§T37](codys-cookbook.md#t37-salt-brining-and-the-dry-brine--codys-standing-practice-heavy-every-time), the Roberts Way); next day a **yellow mustard slather** as glue, **[§4.108 Meathead's Memphis Dust](codys-cookbook.md#4108-meatheads-memphis-dust)** applied *"pretty heavily… not super duper thick, but it's covered — all the crevices,"* and **fresh rosemary ground on top of the rub** — recorded as deliberate *extra* rosemary, not a substitution, since §4.108 already carries 2 tsp crushed dried rosemary (verified against its ingredient list); **225°F, fat side up, no flip, no wrap, no mop** — his reason kept: fat side down sticks to the grate and *"I'm too lazy, I don't even turn it"*; **203°F internal** — *"that's the trick… 203 guarantees that all of the collagen melts into liquid, into savory liquid. **(2)** **Morton vs. Diamond Crystal is a ~1.5× difference by volume**, so since he salts by feel from a bowl and specified Morton, the brand is stated in the ingredient line and a Diamond Crystal user is told to use **more** by volume, not less ([§T106](codys-cookbook.md#t106-salts-stocks--broths-sugars-vinegars-winebeerspirits-and-zest--pantry-reference-the-meathead-method) already fixes Morton Coarse Kosher as this book's reference salt); **(3)** his **fat-side-up reasoning is correct for the right reason** — fat does *not* melt down through the meat and baste it, a myth already busted at [§T40](codys-cookbook.md#t40-the-fat-cap--trim-or-not) and linked rather than restated, so the grate-sticking argument he gives is the actual one. **203°F vs. 195°F handled by keeping every entry as its own:** [§6.2](codys-cookbook.md#62-pulled-pork) and [§6.6](codys-cookbook.md#66-north-carolina-pulled-pork-karmel-version) keep their sources' printed 195°F and [§7.137](codys-cookbook.md#7137-tuffy-stones-championship-pork-butt) its competition figures; Cody's three-way framing kept in his own words — **a pan, or on the Big Green Egg the convector: "a stone, a large cooking stone that separates, and it heats up. Pan-barrier route cross-linked to [§T52](codys-cookbook.md#t52-water-pans-and-drip-pans) with its placement rule restated — **water pan over the heat source, drip pan under the food**. **[§T34 Two-Zone Fire Setup](codys-cookbook.md#t34-two-zone-fire-setup) and §T109 are now explicitly the two halves of one idea** — T34 is fire on one side, T109 is a barrier in between — with a reciprocal one-line pointer added to T34; T34's lid rule is linked, not repeated. Also covered: why it matters in food terms (no flare-ups, nothing scorched on the fire side, a temperature you can hold for hours, and the **¾-inch / sugar-bearing-rub** threshold that makes it mandatory); **what still needs direct heat** — searing, thin cuts, the final crisping pass, with the *indirect the whole way, short direct finish* pattern pointed at [§7.131 Smoked Sous-Vide Steak](codys-cookbook.md#7131-smoked-sous-vide-steak), [§7.134 Afterburner Fajitas](codys-cookbook.md#7134-afterburner-fajitas)'s chimney sear, and [§7.138 Real Fried Chicken on a Gas Grill](codys-cookbook.md#7138-real-fried-chicken-on-a-gas-grill-its-safe); and the practical note that **ceramic is slow up and slow down, so you set the vents and let a kamado settle before the food goes on** — which is why Cody's Egg gets about an hour — while a kettle or gas-grill pan heats fast and holds less steady. **Raised because of [§7.9 Caveman Chicken Wings](codys-cookbook.md#79-caveman-chicken-wings), which cook indirect at 400°F and never said so:** §7.9 now carries §T109 on its *Techniques used* line and a convEGGtor-in instruction in Step 2. Kenji López-Alt's baking-powder wing method, corroborated on rack timing by Food Network and Chili Pepper Madness; all three cited by name and URL in the entry). **Rated 🟢 Easy · Prep ~15 min · Cook ~50 min**, with the overnight fridge dry-brine and the Egg's hour-long warm-up noted as lead time, not cook time, per [CLAUDE.md §3a](CLAUDE.md) — nothing in the recipe can go from perfect to ruined. **The name is kept but glossed:** *caveman* in barbecue usually means cooking straight on the coals; this is Cody's own name for a rack method, marked as cookbook gloss. **The three wing recipes are now cross-linked both ways** — §7.9, [§7.120 Blasphemy Buffalo Chicken Wings](codys-cookbook.md#7120-blasphemy-buffalo-chicken-wings), and [§7.139 Extra Crispy Chicken Wings Without Frying](codys-cookbook.md#7139-extra-crispy-chicken-wings-without-frying) each now point at the others. "4 box" = 4 lb is right for 8–12 dozen, 2–3 lb for six; black pepper and oregano added as Drago's seasoning, all card items kept. **[§9.30 Muffelatta](codys-cookbook.md#930-muffelatta-poboy)** — per-quarter proportions, and the cheese list corrected to ¼ lb provolone plus ¼ lb mozzarella to match Step 3 (Central Grocery, Kitchn). **[§9.31 Quesadillas](codys-cookbook.md#931-quesadillas)** — ½ cup cheese and ⅓ cup beans per 10-in tortilla, medium heat 2–3 + 1–2 min; two bags of cheese for a bag of tortillas (Kitchn, Isabel Eats). **Canal House wording checks settled, none changing the cooked result:** [§7.40 Shrimp Risotto](codys-cookbook.md#740-shrimp-risotto) (broth order kept), [§8.9 Duck Soup](codys-cookbook.md#89-duck-soup-with-cabbage-ham--chinese-rice-noodles) (transitions kept; | 2026-09-07 | **Roux difficulty split into three tiers, per Cody — "use judgment on the roux because some is dark and some is light … a bisque is a light version which is less hard."** The rule, now written into the [Difficulty & Time Index](codys-cookbook.md#difficulty--time-index) intro and [§T1 How to Make a Roux](codys-cookbook.md#t1-how-to-make-a-roux): a **dark roux** (dark brown, dark red-brown, chocolate, mahogany, black) is 🔴 Hard; a **brown or medium roux** (peanut butter, copper, "brown," "medium brown," "light brown") is 🟡 Medium; a **blond, light, or white roux** isn't a Hard-earning step at all. The color target sets the tier — not the quantity, not the pace, not the size of the recipe — and a roux never lowers a rating another risky technique earns. Applied to every roux-based entry in the book, in both directions. **Re-rated 🔴→🟡 (six):** [§4.71 Sauce Brune](codys-cookbook.md#471-sauce-brune-flour-based-brown-sauce--brown-sauce-1) and [§4.72 Sauce Ragoût](codys-cookbook.md#472-sauce-ragoût-flour-based-brown-sauce-with-giblets--brown-sauce-2) (golden nut brown, 8–10 min at moderately low heat), [§8.19 Crawfish Bisque](codys-cookbook.md#819-crawfish-bisque-bisque-décrevisses) (medium red-brown), [§8.42 Creole Courtbouillon](codys-cookbook.md#842-creole-courtbouillon) (medium brown, 2 Tbsp flour), [§8.43 Crawfish Bisque II](codys-cookbook.md#843-crawfish-bisque-ii) ("brown," per p. 59), and [§8.44 Courtbouillon](codys-cookbook.md#844-courtbouillon) ("brown"). **Re-rated 🟡→🔴 (one):** [§8.25 Wild Goose Gumbo with Oysters](codys-cookbook.md#825-wild-goose-gumbo-with-oysters) — the page's target is dark brown; low heat slows the run-up but doesn't change the tier. **Examined and left 🔴 (dark on the page):** [§T1](codys-cookbook.md#t1-how-to-make-a-roux), [§4.12](codys-cookbook.md#412-fish-brown-sauce), [§7.26](codys-cookbook.md#726-crawfish-magnifique-in-a-mirliton-pirogue), [§8.3](codys-cookbook.md#83-chicken-and-andouille-smoked-sausage-gumbo), [§8.5](codys-cookbook.md#85-cajun-seafood-gumbo-with-andouille-smoked-sausage), [§8.23](codys-cookbook.md#823-duck--sausage-gumbo), [§8.24](codys-cookbook.md#824-duck-and-guinea-gumbo), [§8.41](codys-cookbook.md#841-crawfish-bisque-i), [§8.46](codys-cookbook.md#846-vernas-galimatias). **Examined and left 🟡:** [§4.17](codys-cookbook.md#417-giblet-gravy), [§5.29](codys-cookbook.md#529-smothered-mustard-greens), [§7.65](codys-cookbook.md#765-crawfish-pie-i), [§8.26](codys-cookbook.md#826-felecias-goose-gumbo) ("dark golden brown" — read as the brown tier, the operative word being *golden*), [§8.27](codys-cookbook.md#827-green-gumbo), [§8.28](codys-cookbook.md#828-chicken-gumbo-filé), [§8.29](codys-cookbook.md#829-mrs-mcginns-seafood-gumbo), [§8.31](codys-cookbook.md#831-turtle-soup-ii-monteleone). its two-job summary (ballast against wind and lid-opening; humidity that "has a smaller effect than commonly claimed") was unprinted and is replaced by the page's seven listed functions; hot-water-only, fill-to-below-the-lip, above-the-hottest-spot, and the Weber Smokey Mountain note added. **(2) [§T49 Lookin' Ain't Cookin'](codys-cookbook.md#t49-lookin-aint-cookin--and-other-lid-myths) had a figure wrong and a theory invented** — the charcoal kettle opened 1 minute recovered in *about 2 minutes*, not 10; the air-temperature drop is barely noticeable in a large smoker, 10°F or more in a small one) is on p. 63, and the section continues on an unphotographed p. 64; a "full smoker recovers more slowly after the lid opens" bullet that contradicts §T49 is struck, the other two bullets are labeled cookbook gloss, and the thickness rule is credited to [§T42](codys-cookbook.md#t42-what-controls-cooking-time). **(4) [§T47 Cooking Vegetables and Fruits](codys-cookbook.md#t47-cooking-vegetables-and-fruits--and-reverse-searing-them-too) had an entire fabricated paragraph** — peach halves, pineapple rings, bananas, "a couple of minutes," an under-a-minute burn window — where p. 67 says only that fruits and seeds taste better grilled; a couple-of-minutes-a-side finish, a burns-before-the-center-softens rationale, and a crowd-the-pan line were also unprinted; the composition claim is restated in the page's terms and the quick-cooking list is the page's. **(5) [§T48 Basting and Spritzing](codys-cookbook.md#t48-basting-and-spritzing--and-whats-really-oozing-out-of-the-meat)'s ooze sidebar had been rewritten from memory** — the egg-white analogy and "nothing worse than a bit overcooked" are unprinted; the p. 58 remedies (brine the salmon, wipe it off, paint with an acidic wash), Dr. Antonio Mata's burger explanation, and "perfectly safe to eat" are restored; oil on skin *sometimes* helps; the smoke-sticks-to-wet-surfaces line added; the thin-mop paragraph credited to p. 178 and its invented mechanism struck. **(6) [§T50 Saucing Strategies](codys-cookbook.md#t50-saucing-strategies--when-how-much-and-food-safety)** said a homemade sauce keeps "for weeks" — p. 176 says *months*, and its point is preservatives versus none, not room-temperature shelf life; an unprinted "thick coat scorches before it sets" struck; the skip-the-sauce option, the broiler's back-side-first, the offset-firebox grate, the gas grill, the ~$30 butane torch, canning jars, and the dried-herbs note added; every figure (320°F, 30 minutes, 5 minutes a side, 5–10 minutes broiling, two coats max) confirmed. **(7) [§T53 The Science of a Good Rub](codys-cookbook.md#t53-the-science-of-a-good-rub--three-ss-no-salt-in-rubs-and-storage)** — the analogy is *gas pedal, brake, and clutch*; the rice tip is dry the rice first, not "a few grains"; pepper is ground fresh *always*, the sifting trick is for hot grilling, and "coarse" was unprinted; the salt-by-weight / spices-by-surface-area principle and the after-cooking-taste note added; an invented "the one S that overwhelms the others" struck. **(8) [§T46 Freezing and Reheating Leftovers](codys-cookbook.md#t46-freezing-and-reheating-leftovers)** — the broiler step is 5 to 10 minutes on *one* side, then turn and broil a few more minutes, not "per side"; "portion into meal-sized amounts" and "quality drifts after a few months" were unprinted; the page's per-person planning amounts and the make-ahead-ribs only-if-you-must caveat added; every other figure confirmed. **No salt guidance changed anywhere** — the no-salt-rub warnings and Cody's brine-heavy rule stand as they were. Mumbo](codys-cookbook.md#4123-dc-mumbo-sauce) prints **2⅔ cups / less than an hour** (badge said 30); [§4.129 Board Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#4129-sage-and-thyme-board-sauce) prints **about ½ cup for 2 lb of meat / 30 minutes** (badge said 10). **Method and wording fixes:** [§4.117](codys-cookbook.md#4117-kansas-city-classic-barbecue-sauce)'s tamarind is found in an *Indian* or Asian grocery, not Italian; [§4.118](codys-cookbook.md#4118-columbia-gold-south-carolina-mustard-sauce) loses an unprinted "can be used right away"; [§4.121](codys-cookbook.md#4121-texas-mop-sauce)'s headnote no longer claims "no ketchup" in a sauce that calls for ¼ cup of it; [§4.122](codys-cookbook.md#4122-bacon-and-onion-jam) regains the page's cook-the-bacon-in-two-batches-if-necessary and loses an unprinted "stirring occasionally"; [§4.124 Tartar Sauce](codys-cookbook.md#4124-tartar-sauce)'s Notes now say tarragon is essential and minced celery is an addition rather than a caper substitute; [§4.129](codys-cookbook.md#4129-sage-and-thyme-board-sauce)'s Step 1 adds the black pepper only, since salt is optional on the page, and the vessel is a coffee cup; [§4.130 Chimichurri](codys-cookbook.md#4130-chimichurri-sauce)'s chef is **Ryan Udvett** (was "Udette"); [§4.131 Pesto](codys-cookbook.md#4131-pesto)'s "not authentic Genovese" remark is re-attached to the olives, where the page puts it; [§4.175](codys-cookbook.md#4175-teriyaki-sauce)'s Sriracha Note says the small quantity doesn't make the sauce very hot, and its scorch and end-of-cook glossing is labeled as this cookbook's own. | 2026-09-07 | **Independent second-pass re-verification of the eleven Chicken & Turkey–chapter entries from the 54-page *Meathead: The Science of Great Barbecue and Grilling* scan — [§7.118 Simon & Garfunkel Chicken](codys-cookbook.md#7118-simon--garfunkel-chicken) through [§7.122 The Ultimate Smoked Turkey](codys-cookbook.md#7122-the-ultimate-smoked-turkey) and [§T77 Tips on Cooking Poultry](codys-cookbook.md#t77-tips-on-cooking-poultry) through [§T82 How to Carve a Turkey](codys-cookbook.md#t82-how-to-carve-a-turkey) — every figure (temperature, time, weight, percentage, thickness), every claim, and every step in its printed order read again against book pp. 294–298, 301–304, and 306–313 themselves (file indexes 31–40, the embedded photos pulled at native 2500-pixel resolution and cropped a column at a time, the sideways p. 304 turned upright), without leaning on the 2026-09-05 transcription's notes.** The recipes were committed in three earlier runs today; this run finished the six technique entries. **The chapter followed the book's pattern to the letter: the recipes were right in every quantity and wrong only in step structure, and all six technique sidebars carried figures or advice the page does not print.** Ranked by how much it changes what a cook does: **(1) [§T81 Cooking the Perfect Turkey](codys-cookbook.md#t81-cooking-the-perfect-turkey) was missing a printed rule and had softened another into its opposite** — the page's *Cook It at the Right Temperature: roast the bird as close to 325°F as your cooker will let you* was not in the list at all, and its *Do Not Rest or Tent with Foil* had become "a short rest uncovered is plenty" while its carryover rule (*take the bird off and start carving immediately*) had become "without an unnecessarily long rest." Restored to the page; the pop-up bullet's "170°F+ that many older recipes call for" is the page's *170 to 180°F*; unprinted reasons for trussing, basting, stuffing, and racks are struck, and the page's onions-and-oranges, rubbery-skin, apple-juice, "August or October," and above-a-roasting-pan (its p. 252 illustration — [§T67](codys-cookbook.md#t67-ban-the-v-shaped-rack)) lines are restored. **(2) [§T77 Tips on Cooking Poultry](codys-cookbook.md#t77-tips-on-cooking-poultry) had two invented figures** — dark meat "in the 165–175°F range" where the page prints *about 170°F* (about 10 percent fat), and an indirect side at "325–350°F" where the page reverse-sears parts and halves *starting at 325°F* and reserves 350 to 400°F for the oven or hot grill you move the bird to if the skin isn't crisp at 145°F — and had dropped the white-meat reasoning (about 6 percent fat, best at 155°F but 10°F under the USDA line, so 160°F and carryover to 165°F), the reverse sear's *not quite done, about 145°F* pull point and lid-down / lid-up instruction, and the wings-finish-first rule. Its explanations were not the page's either: the spatchcock warning had grown a story about legs "falling off the direct side into the flame," roasting whole was blamed on thigh-versus-breast temperatures (the page: all the heat comes from outside and the breasts are thick and unforgiving), halving was "a simpler cut" (the page: the thighs tear off and the wings cook at a different rate), and "a rotisserie is worth owning" was unprinted. The vertical roast's "chimney" trick, the cut-it-up-yourself-for-stock paragraph, and the *Going Skinless* and *Smoking Poultry* paragraphs are added. **(3) [§T82 How to Carve a Turkey](codys-cookbook.md#t82-how-to-carve-a-turkey) had two joints misdescribed, a step reversed, a step missing, and the page's only figure absent** — the hip was placed "between the thigh and drumstick" (the page: where the thigh meets the body; the knee is the drumstick joint you flex first), the thigh was boned "from the skin side" (the page: skin side *down*, knife around and under the bone, then skin side up to slice), the wings step (shoulder joint, picture 5) was gone, the breast slices are *at least ¼ inch thick*, and the turkey oysters were moved to Step 3 with an unprinted spoon — restored to the page's place after the breasts, right under each shoulder blade, popped out with the fingers; the wishbone, the teardrop-shaped hunk, the thumb-and-forefinger skin technique, and *reassemble to keep it warm and wet* are restored, and "pass the rest of the gravy at the table" was unprinted. **(4) [§T80 Choosing Your Turkey](codys-cookbook.md#t80-choosing-your-turkey) had the yield wrong** — "roughly ⅓ pound of cooked meat per person" where the page prints *about ½ pound per person* — and had run two paragraphs together: the page's "almost mission impossible" is about finding an *unsalted* supermarket bird (special-order, a specialty store like Whole Foods, or a farmer), not a fresh one, and the USDA definition of "fresh" (never taken below **26°F**, *the USDA is allowing marketers to deceive the public*) was missing; a truly fresh bird must be killed *within a week of the date you will consume it*, and two small birds are *more tender and juicy*, not "more evenly" cooked. 1 pound per person, 8 percent, kosher birds, and 24 hours per 4 pounds confirmed. **(5) [§T78 Beer Can Chicken](codys-cookbook.md#t78-myth--beer-can-chicken-is-the-best-way-to-cook-a-bird) had the page's numbers replaced with a gloss and its safety point replaced with an unprinted one** — "nowhere near its 212°F boiling point" is the page's *meat 70 percent water, beer more than 92, a single thermal mass; at 160°F in the chicken the beer will not rise above 150°F, 62°F below boiling*; the "unstable footing on a hot grill" risk is the page's *the center of the bird is cooled by the can, so the risk of undercooking is high*, plus the burn from wiggling it out; "halved citrus" was unprinted; the 92-percent / 5-percent figures now carry the page's attribution (Scott Bruslind, Analysis Laboratory), and the popular-procedure and white-tablecloth "final proof" paragraphs are added. **(6) [§T79 Anatomy of a Chicken Wing](codys-cookbook.md#t79-anatomy-of-a-chicken-wing)** had invented every description — "miniature drumstick," "two thin bones," "mostly skin, cartilage, and little bones," and tips that "burn long before the rest is done" — where the sidebar prints only the three pieces, their positions, that they cook and finish at different rates, and that the author serves only the drumettes and wingettes; rewritten to the page. **The recipes (committed earlier today):** [§7.118](codys-cookbook.md#7118-simon--garfunkel-chicken) lost an unprinted rest-then-serve Step 4 and gained its MAKES/TAKES line (2–3 hours start to finish); [§7.119](codys-cookbook.md#7119-big-bob-gibsons-chicken-in-bama-white-sauce) lost a "dark brown, not black" target that belongs to Cornell Chicken, corrected its shopping list to the printed 3 to 4 pounds, and points at [§4.172](codys-cookbook.md#4172-alabama-white-sauce); [§7.120](codys-cookbook.md#7120-blasphemy-buffalo-chicken-wings) was exact and regained four dropped sentences; [§7.121](codys-cookbook.md#7121-marinated-cornish-game-hens) had two wrong figures — the move to direct heat at **145°F**, not 140, and hens younger than **five** weeks, not two; [§7.122](codys-cookbook.md#7122-the-ultimate-smoked-turkey) was exact in every figure, renumbered to the printed eleven steps, and its Prep restated to the printed 20 minutes with the badge and Difficulty & Time Index row updated, celery corrected to the printed 1 stalk. [§T82](codys-cookbook.md#t82-how-to-carve-a-turkey)'s *Used in* now points at §7.122's Step 11, where the carving reference actually sits after that renumbering. **No salt quantity was altered anywhere; no difficulty rating changed; no §17 cut or method changed.** Nothing on any of the six spreads was illegible — p. 308's lower left column and p. 312's lower left column sit under glare bands but read cleanly at native resolution. Every entry carries a dated ✅ line. |
| 2026-09-07 | **Independent second-pass re-verification of the five Ground Meats–chapter entries from the 54-page *Meathead: The Science of Great Barbecue and Grilling* scan — [§7.115 The Great American Steakhouse Steakburger](codys-cookbook.md#7115-the-great-american-steakhouse-steakburger) and [§T73 Burger Basics](codys-cookbook.md#t73-burger-basics--fat-content-grind-and-handling) through [§T76 Cooking Sausages](codys-cookbook.md#t76-cooking-sausages--precooked-vs-raw-temperature-targets) — every ingredient line, every step in its printed order, every time, temperature, percentage, and MAKES/TAKES line, and every city and topping read again against book pp. 268–271 and 276–277 themselves (file indexes 27–29, the embedded photos pulled at native 2500-pixel resolution and cropped a quadrant at a time), without leaning on the 2026-09-05 transcription's notes.** The chapter followed the book's pattern exactly: **the recipe's numbers were right and its steps were not, and all four technique entries carried fabricated figures or reversed advice.** Ranked by how much it changes what a cook does: **(1) [§T76 Cooking Sausages](codys-cookbook.md#t76-cooking-sausages--precooked-vs-raw-temperature-targets) had both of its central claims backwards** — it said *most supermarket sausage is sold precooked, around 75 percent of it* (the page: *some are precooked, but **most are sold raw***; no percentage is printed) and told the cook to keep sausages *mostly on the indirect side and roll them onto the direct side at the end* (the page: *you will cook over direct heat most of the time*, with the direct zone's heat kept down and the indirect zone as a safe zone or to hold finished links). Rewritten to the page; the flare-up and looks-done-outside rationales were unprinted and are struck; 25 percent fat, 140°F precooked, 160°F uncooked, and the thermometer-through-the-end rule confirmed; the bend-it-flat, parallel-to-the-grates, and don't-let-them-split lines restored. **(2) [§7.115](codys-cookbook.md#7115-the-great-american-steakhouse-steakburger) had eight steps where the page prints nine, and four departed from the page:** the bacon was cooked *crispy is the goal* where the page says *until the bacon is the desired doneness. I like it bendable*; the printed Step 5 — thermometer into the side of both burgers, *turn them over, sprinkle with salt, and cook for another 10 minutes* — was dropped, so the second side never got its salt and the indirect stage ran 10 minutes short; the sear was written *to at least 160°F* where the page says *remove the burgers when the interiors are 5°F below the desired temperature*, with the lid up, flares moved away from, and a gas / charcoal / pellet-griddle paragraph that was missing; and the build was stacked upside down — the page puts the bacon on the glop, the burger on the bacon, and the lettuce, onion, and tomato *on top of the patty* (*you have eight fingers to manage the top of the bun*), in a bowl. The page's Step 9 (elbows on the table) replaces an unprinted *serve right away*. **Every quantity was exact** — the three ¼-teaspoon seasonings, 1 lb chuck at 25% fat, about 1 Tbsp butter, 2 buns, 4 bacon slices, **1 teaspoon kosher salt (unchanged)**, 2 Tbsp Burger Glop (the page cites its p. 188, which is [§4.128](codys-cookbook.md#4128-burger-glop)), 2 lettuce leaves, 2 onion slices about ⅛ inch, 2 tomato slices, the 225°F indirect target, the 20°F-below threshold — as were the printed MAKES 2 servings / TAKES about 1 hour and the Notes. The heading is renamed to the page's red-box title, ***Steakburger***, with every anchor updated; start to finish restated from ~50 min to the printed 1 hour (Prep + Cook unchanged, so the Difficulty & Time Index row keeps its place); the 160°F ground-beef floor stays only as a labeled cookbook note under Step 7, credited to p. 268 where the book prints it. **(3) [§T74 Flavoring the Burger](codys-cookbook.md#t74-flavoring-the-burger--salting-timing-and-the-weight-loss-test) had every number in the weight-loss test wrong** — 30 / 28 / 22 percent where the graphic prints **31% (no salt), 30% (salt on surface), 21% (salt mixed in)**, cleanly legible, so the *read off a small graphic* caveat is struck — and its reasoning reversed: the page recommends salting the exterior because mixed-in salt *compacted the meat and it became dense and rubbery*, not because a Maillard crust *matters more than the last few percentage points of moisture*. The 80%-lean / 325°F test conditions, Bobby Flay's *that's meatloaf* quote, the burns-on-the-surface reason for mixing in garlic and onion powder, the avoid-wine-and-Worcestershire rule, the 6-to-8-ounce and ½-to-¾-inch sizing, and the no-dimple paragraph restored; an invented egg-and-breadcrumb contamination line struck. **(4) [§T75 Regional Hot Dogs](codys-cookbook.md#t75-regional-hot-dogs--a-cross-country-survey) had merged two dogs into one and invented toppings for three more** — the *Cincinnati/Detroit Coney* with its *cinnamon-and-clove-spiced meat sauce* (no spice is printed) is now the page's pork-and-beef Cincinnati Cheese Coney and the skinless-beef Detroit Coney with its beef-heart, no-beans chili; New York City's Coney Island Hot Dog (spicy brown mustard, sauerkraut, griddled onions) was missing entirely; the Chicago Dog is *simmered* (not *steamed or water-boiled*) with tomato *slices* and a *kosher* pickle spear under the *seven ingredients, no more, no less* rule; *cream cheese and grilled onions* for Seattle, a *garlic-and-relish* Puka Dog, and a *Ripper* nickname for the *North Jersey* Italian Dog are all unprinted and struck; the Texas Wiener and Sonoran Hot Dog restored to the name list; the *pick one region's full topping list* takeaway was unprinted. **(5) [§T73 Burger Basics](codys-cookbook.md#t73-burger-basics--fat-content-grind-and-handling)** said preground chuck is *closer to 15 percent fat* (the page: *15 to 20 percent*) and explained oxidation and coarse grinds with sentences the page doesn't print; the page's chuck-steak-plus-white-fat method, *don't waste money on ribeye or Wagyu*, the cheap-cuts list, the ¼-inch once-through grind, the ground-round (10–15) and ground-sirloin (5–10) figures, the suet tip, and the 30-second boiling-water surface pasteurization for burgers cooked under 160°F are all added. [§17 Meats & Cuts](codys-cookbook.md#17-meats--cuts-reference-index)' ground-beef row, the TOC line, and both technique *Used in* lines carry the new title. **No salt quantity was altered; no difficulty rating changed.** Nothing on any of the three spreads was illegible — p. 268's lower left column and p. 276's Coney Island paragraph sit under glare bands but read cleanly at native resolution. Every entry carries a dated ✅ line. |
| 2026-09-07 | **Independent second-pass re-verification of the eight Beef-chapter technique entries from the 54-page *Meathead: The Science of Great Barbecue and Grilling* scan — [§T65 Steaks](codys-cookbook.md#t65-steaks--grades-cuts-and-matching-temperature-to-thickness) through [§T72 Steaming Pastrami](codys-cookbook.md#t72-steaming-pastrami) — every figure (temperature, time, weight, thickness, percentage, dimension), every claim, and every cross-reference to §7.106–§7.114 read again against book pp. 242–243, 247–250, 252, 256, 258–259, and 262–265 themselves (file indexes 16–27, the embedded photos pulled at full resolution and cropped a column at a time), without leaning on the 2026-09-05 transcription's notes.** As with the pork sidebars, the recipes were exact and the technique entries were not: **six of the eight carried figures or advice the page does not print, and one had an instruction reversed.** Ranked by how much it changes what a cook does: **(1) [§T72 Steaming Pastrami](codys-cookbook.md#t72-steaming-pastrami) said "if the pan is steel, keep the foil off the *metal*"** — the page says *don't let the foil touch the **meat***; the salt, water, steel, and aluminum together are what can melt the foil (the "lasagna effect"). Corrected, and the page's time-depends-on-thickness-and-water-heat note added. **(2) [§T66 Prime Rib & Rib Roast](codys-cookbook.md#t66-prime-rib--rib-roast--ordering-bones-and-carving) claimed the bones get *tied back onto the roast for presentation* and cited [§7.114](codys-cookbook.md#7114-prime-rib) Step 3** — neither page prints it; Step 3 only ties the boneless roast round. Struck, and p. 248's actual bones paragraph is now there: a bone-in roast is a "standing rib roast," buy bone-in only if it's cheaper per pound, have the butcher remove the bones or do it yourself, and *they make a delicious second meal*. The page's **7 bones**, **20 percent shrinkage**, five separate reasons for deboning (crust and carving had been merged), the rib cap's *goes to well-done, so grill it separately* reason, and p. 247's list of other roasts the method suits and the cheaper cuts that come close are all added. **(3) [§T68 Beef Ribs](codys-cookbook.md#t68-beef-ribs--the-long-and-the-short-of-them) had three figures wrong** — "bones run 1 to 2 inches apart" (the page: *1 to 2 inches of meat on top*), "2- to 3-bone sections" (the page: *riblets 2 to 3 inches long*; slabs are 4 bones), and an 8-by-9-inch short plate (the page: *8 by 8*) — plus an unprinted "tender end of the steer" reason and "leaner" for back ribs; the page says *treat back ribs the same as short ribs* and that they can finish in *as little as 3 hours*. Rewritten to the page; the 2-pound bone and hardworking-muscle lines are credited to [§7.110](codys-cookbook.md#7110-short-ribs-texas-style)'s p. 257 where they're actually printed. **(4) [§T69 Brisket Basics](codys-cookbook.md#t69-brisket-basics--anatomy-of-a-whole-packer-brisket) said "the flat is rectangular"** — the page: *the flat is pointy and the point is oval. Go figure.* Struck; the lean side is *close to fat-free*, not "none"; the page's **60 percent** weight-bearing figure, the three butcher cuts (packer; first-cut flat; second-cut point or deckle), the silverskin, the flat's **¼-to-1½-inch** and the point's **over-4-inch** thickness, the diagram's muscle names, and the handpick-the-marbling / unlabeled-is-Select rule are added. **(5) [§T65 Steaks](codys-cookbook.md#t65-steaks--grades-cuts-and-matching-temperature-to-thickness) carried an unprinted "gray, overcooked band" explanation and an unprinted "over 1½ inches" threshold** — the page's rule is only *the thicker the steak, the lower the cooking temperature*; | 2026-09-07 | **[§14.36 Scented Cream](codys-cookbook.md#1436-scented-cream) added from *Salt, Fat, Acid, Heat* pp. 422–425 — one entry that closes five standing gaps and retires every whipped-cream stand-in in the book.** Cody photographed both spreads (pp. 422–423 and 424–425), which the 2026-09-06 page-order audit had verified as absent from the original 50-page scan. **The base recipe is tiny — 1 cup chilled heavy cream and 1½ teaspoons granulated sugar** — with a chilled deep metal bowl and whisk (20 min in the freezer), the flavoring stirred or steeped in, then whisked **by hand** to soft peaks; by machine, low speed only, and switch to a handheld whisk to finish. Leftovers keep 2 days and deflated cream re-whisks back to soft peaks. **The important structural finding: "Vanilla Cream" is not a recipe.** It is one line inside this one — ¼ vanilla bean's scraped seeds, or 1 teaspoon vanilla extract, stirred in before whipping — and the same is true of Cardamom, Caramel, Chocolate, Coffee, and Tangy Whipped Cream. The entry is therefore structured as **one base recipe plus 19 listed flavor options in the source's own four groups** (20 named creams, since Toasted Almond and Toasted Hazelnut share a line), following the variation-list precedent already set at [§5.49 Persian-ish Rice](codys-cookbook.md#549-persian-ish-rice) and [§14.25 Marshmallowy Meringues](codys-cookbook.md#1425-marshmallowy-meringues) rather than spinning any of them into separate numbered recipes: **Group 1, stirred in just before whipping (9)** — Vanilla, Spiced (cardamom/cinnamon/nutmeg), Lemon, Orange, Rose, Orange-Flower, Boozy, Almond, Coffee; **Group 2, simmer-steeped in half the cream, strained, chilled, remaining cream added (3)** — Peach Leaf (12 torn leaves, 15 min), Earl Grey (2 Tbsp leaves, 10 min), Bay Leaf (6 torn leaves, 15 min); **Group 3, cold-steeped 2 hr to overnight, then strained (3)** — *Noyau* apricot kernel, Toasted Almond or Hazelnut, Toasted Coconut; **Group 4, four with their own methods** — Chocolate (scalded cream over 2 oz bittersweet chocolate), Caramel (¼ cup sugar cooked to dark amber, stopped with cream, pinch of salt), Tangy Whipped (a half batch, with ¼ cup sour cream, Greek yogurt, or crème fraîche and 3 Tbsp sugar), and dairy-free Coconut (the solid fat off two cans of full-fat coconut milk). **Rated 🟡 Medium, not 🔴 Hard**, per [CLAUDE.md §3a](CLAUDE.md)'s technique-risk test. Overwhipping breaks the emulsion into butter and that is genuinely unrecoverable — the source's own p. 423 illustration puts *"smooth as velvet"* and *"3 seconds from butter"* in adjacent frames — **but the source hands you an explicit guard against it**, whip by hand, which stretches those three seconds into thirty and lets you feel the cream stiffen before you can see it. Unrecoverable but well telegraphed, the same reasoning that holds [§14.25](codys-cookbook.md#1425-marshmallowy-meringues) and [§14.35 All-Butter Pie Dough](codys-cookbook.md#1435-all-butter-pie-dough) at Medium. Per Cody's standing instruction, the prose explains soft peaks against stiff and past-stiff, the fat-globule mechanism by which whipping becomes butter-making, why a hand whisk guards against it, why the bowl and whisk go in the freezer, and why the headnote warns off carrageenan-stabilized and UHT cream. **Seven retrofits, every one of them a flag retired rather than reworded:** [§14.21 Lori's Chocolate Midnight Cake](codys-cookbook.md#1421-loris-chocolate-midnight-cake) and [§14.23 Fresh Ginger and Molasses Cake](codys-cookbook.md#1423-fresh-ginger-and-molasses-cake) (2 cups Vanilla Cream each — exactly one batch; ingredient line, fill step, substitute note, grocery list, nutrition note and Open Questions all repointed, and their ⚠️ TOC flags dropped); [§14.25 Marshmallowy Meringues](codys-cookbook.md#1425-marshmallowy-meringues) (Scented Cream in Pavlovas, Cardamom Cream in Persian Pavlovas, Vanilla Cream in Meringue Fool, Caramel Cream in Chocolate-Caramel Meringue Fool — four references, all now live links); [§14.26 Classic Pumpkin Pie](codys-cookbook.md#1426-classic-pumpkin-pie) (Tangy Whipped, Vanilla, Cinnamon and Caramel Cream, plus the Chocolate Pudding Pie variation's cream list); [§14.27 Light and Flaky Buttermilk Biscuits](codys-cookbook.md#1427-light-and-flaky-buttermilk-biscuits) (the Shortcakes filling); [§14.29 Roast It on a Bed of Fig Leaves](codys-cookbook.md#1429-roast-it-on-a-bed-of-fig-leaves) (its serving suggestion — and the source's own **Bay Leaf Cream atop roasted peaches** pairing turns out to be this exact dish, now cross-linked both ways); and [§14.34 Poach It in Wine](codys-cookbook.md#1434-poach-it-in-wine). **Only the Strawberry/fruit Compote (p. 407) and Rose Scented Berries gaps remain open at those entries, and both are on Cody's explicitly-dropped list.** **Named as undocumented, and deliberately NOT added to any tracking list** — Cody has said he isn't pursuing them: the source's **Apple and Frangipane Tart** (p. 397), **Classic Apple Pie** (p. 388), **Bittersweet Chocolate Pudding** (p. 416), and **Jasmine Rice** (p. 282). **Two the source cross-references from these pages *are* already here**, and are now linked instead of flagged: its **Coffee Granita** is [§14.28](codys-cookbook.md#1428-juice-it-and-make-granita-orange-granita-coffee-granita-variation)'s variation and its **Chocolate Pudding Pie** is [§14.26](codys-cookbook.md#1426-classic-pumpkin-pie)'s. Nutrition estimated for the base (~835 kcal a batch, ~105 per ¼-cup dollop) with the four Group 4 variations costed separately, since they shift it substantially. **Nothing on either page was illegible.** TOC and Difficulty & Time Index updated. |
| 2026-09-06 | **Two *Salt, Fat, Acid, Heat* recipes recovered, and the technique entry that had been written from a partial read is now complete.** Cody photographed three book pages this project had verified as genuinely absent from the 50-page scan — **pp. 379, 385, and 386** — and all three are now transcribed. **[§4.182 Basic Pepper Paste](codys-cookbook.md#4182-basic-pepper-paste)** (book p. 379) — 3 ounces of dried guajillo, New Mexico, Anaheim, or ancho chilies, stemmed and seeded, rehydrated 30 to 60 minutes under 4 cups of boiling water with a plate holding them down, then blended at least 3 minutes with ¾ cup extra-virgin olive oil and salt, thinned if needed with ¼ cup of the reserved soaking water, sieved if it won't come smooth after 5 minutes, and stored under oil for 10 days or frozen for 3 months. Rated **🟢 Easy** per [CLAUDE.md §3a](CLAUDE.md) — nothing on a burner, a forgiving 30-minute soak window, and the one thing that can go wrong (a paste that won't come smooth) has a printed remedy in the recipe itself; the soak is lead time, not difficulty. **The same page's "Pepper Sauce" section intro is folded into that entry as a headnote rather than given its own T-number** — the same practice [§4.164 Pesto](codys-cookbook.md#4164-pesto-basil-pesto) already uses for its section intro, since it's a few sentences of framing for one recipe rather than a technique of its own. It names four world pepper sauces built on this base: **Rouille** (already here at [§4.163](codys-cookbook.md#4163-rouille-pepper-mayonnaise)), **Harissa** (which the source serves alongside its Kufte Kebabs, p. 356 — this cookbook's [§7.123](codys-cookbook.md#7123-kufte-kebabs)), **Romesco**, and **Muhammara**; the last three are named as gaps rather than invented. **[§14.35 All-Butter Pie Dough](codys-cookbook.md#1435-all-butter-pie-dough)** (book p. 386) — 2¼ cups (12 oz) flour, 1 generous tablespoon sugar, a large pinch of salt, 16 tablespoons (8 oz) chilled unsalted butter in ½-inch cubes, about ½ cup ice water, and 1 teaspoon white vinegar, mixed in a stand-mixer bowl that has itself been frozen for 20 minutes; makes **two 10-ounce balls of dough** — two single-crust 9-inch pies, one double-crust, or 1 Chicken Pot Pie. The plastic-wrap flip, the ball-and-cut, the disks, the 2-hour-or-overnight chill, the 2-month freeze (double plastic, then foil), and the source's food-processor/pastry-blender alternative are all transcribed. **Rated 🟡 Medium, deliberately not 🟢:** this dough has two real failure modes and **neither can be undone** — too much water hydrates flour into gluten and there is no way to take water back out, and overmixing smears the butter so the flakes never form at all. What keeps it out of 🔴 Hard is that the source hands you a checkpoint *before* you're committed — the **squeeze test**, performed with the mixer stopped — and neither failure arrives in a ten-second window the way a roux or a caramel does. Same rating and same reasoning as [§14.19 Vodka Pie Crust](codys-cookbook.md#1419-vodka-pie-crust) and [§14.27 Light and Flaky Buttermilk Biscuits](codys-cookbook.md#1427-light-and-flaky-buttermilk-biscuits). **[§T89 Butter-and-Flour Doughs](codys-cookbook.md#t89-butter-and-flour-doughs--weighing-retaining-creaminess-and-breaking-an-emulsion) — completed from the full chapter intro on book p. 385, which it had only ever been written from a partial read of.** Part A is rebuilt from the whole printed page and gains four things it never had: the source's own **"follow the directions as closely as possible"** argument together with its escape hatch (*put your own spin on things with different spices, herbs, or flavors* — change the seasoning, not the structure); its unusually direct **digital-kitchen-scale** recommendation, promising an immediate change in the quality and consistency of your baking; the **spoon-and-sweep method written out as two actual steps** (fill the cup with a separate spoon or scoop without pressing, then level with the straight edge of a knife) instead of a one-line gesture at it; and — the important one — **the actual mechanism for why butter-and-flour doughs are kept cold**, which the earlier version only alluded to: *butter contains water, and if the butter is allowed to melt, that water combines with the flour to make gluten, leading to tough, chewy pastries.* The source's chilly-minded pastry chef, whose ethereal pastries came from discouraging excess gluten at every stage, is credited as printed. **Part B's standing gap is closed** — its forward reference to "All-Butter Pie Dough" now points at [§14.35](codys-cookbook.md#1435-all-butter-pie-dough) instead of naming a verified absence. **EVERY CROSS-REFERENCE THAT WAS STANDING IN FOR EITHER RECIPE IS RETROFITTED.** **[§4.163 Rouille](codys-cookbook.md#4163-rouille-pepper-mayonnaise)** had been built on a **jarred roasted-red-pepper purée** since the day it was added, because p. 379 was confirmed absent — its ingredient line, its explanatory note, and its grocery list now all point at [§4.182](codys-cookbook.md#4182-basic-pepper-paste), with the jarred purée demoted to an explicit "in a pinch" fallback rather than the thing the recipe calls for; a note records that one batch of §4.182 makes about a cup, so a single batch covers §4.163's ⅓ cup three times over. **[§14.26 Classic Pumpkin Pie](codys-cookbook.md#1426-classic-pumpkin-pie)** had been pointing at [§T26 Pâte Brisée](codys-cookbook.md#t26-pâte-brisée--pastry-shells-pie-dough-shaping-and-baking) and [§14.19 Vodka Pie Crust](codys-cookbook.md#1419-vodka-pie-crust) as substitutes for a crust it names by name — its ingredient line, its crust note, its grocery list, and its Open Questions block are all updated to [§14.35](codys-cookbook.md#1435-all-butter-pie-dough), with §T26 and §14.19 kept as genuine alternatives rather than stand-ins for something missing, and the Open Question struck through and marked settled rather than deleted. **The file was searched for every other reference to either component, and the remaining ones are the two TOC annotations** (§14.26's ⚠️ flag retired, §4.163's left alone since it never carried one) **and the historical changelog rows below, which are left as written.** **Jargon explained inline per Cody's standing request:** **spoon-and-sweep** measuring (written out as steps at both [§T89](codys-cookbook.md#t89-butter-and-flour-doughs--weighing-retaining-creaminess-and-breaking-an-emulsion) and [§14.35](codys-cookbook.md#1435-all-butter-pie-dough)'s ingredient list); **why melted butter makes tough pastry** (the butter-contains-water → gluten mechanism, at §T89 and again in §14.35's Step 1); **why the dough is chilled at literally every stage** — bowl, paddle, butter, water, and the finished disks — which follows from that same one fact; **rehydrating dried chilies** (why a soak is not optional, and what a skipped one costs you: gritty flecks of skin instead of a smooth paste) at [§4.182](codys-cookbook.md#4182-basic-pepper-paste) Step 3; and the squeeze test, the walnut-piece butter cue, and why the plastic wrap is used as a tool rather than as a wrapper, all at §14.35. **NO SALT QUANTITY WAS ALTERED.** Both recipes print salt without a measured amount — **"Large pinch of salt"** in the pie dough and a bare **"Salt"** in the pepper paste — and both are transcribed exactly as printed, with the absence flagged in each entry's Open Questions rather than a number invented for either. **Neither recipe prints net carbs, so neither is tagged 🥑 keto** per [CLAUDE.md §3c](CLAUDE.md); both Nutrition blocks are estimated from the ingredient lists and labeled as such, and both account for what's actually eaten (§4.182 excludes the discarded stems, seeds, and soaking water). **Three things are flagged rather than guessed at:** the pie dough's freezing instruction is printed as *"To freeze unwrapped, prepared dough for up to 2 months, double-wrap it in plastic…"* — **"unwrapped" sits oddly against an instruction to wrap it twice, so it is quoted verbatim rather than silently smoothed**; §4.182's printed **MAKES about 1 cup** doesn't reconcile with its own ingredient volumes, since ¾ cup of olive oil alone accounts for most of that before any chile purée goes in, so it is transcribed as printed with the discrepancy named and the nutrition divided by the printed 16 tablespoons; One method detail restored: the **Torn Croutons go into the bowl and get tossed *with* the greens and dressing**, not scattered on afterward, and the tossing happens in a large bowl. **[§8.38 Brothy: Stracciatella (Roman Egg Drop Soup)](codys-cookbook.md#838-brothy-stracciatella-roman-egg-drop-soup) (p. 273): every quantity and step correct as written** — 9 cups stock, 6 large eggs, 1 Tbsp parsley, makes 10 cups (serves 4 to 6), 30-second egg cook, 3-day keep, reheat gently to a simmer. One flag added, no quantity touched: the page prints *"¾-ounce chunk of Parmesan, finely grated (about ¾ cup)"* and both halves were re-read at high magnification to be sure — that really is what's printed, but the two measures don't reconcile with each other (the same book's Caesar prints 3 ounces to about 1 cup, so ¾ cup is a little over 2 oz, and a ¾-ounce chunk would grate to about 3 Tbsp). The printed wording stands in the ingredient list; a note now says to buy and grate to the **¾ cup**, which is what the nutrition figures already assume. **[§14.21 Lori's Chocolate Midnight Cake](codys-cookbook.md#1421-loris-chocolate-midnight-cake) (pp. 410–411): correct as written, no changes** — all ten ingredients match to the ounce (½ cup/2 oz Dutch-process cocoa, 1½ cups/10½ oz sugar, 2 tsp kosher or 1 tsp fine sea salt, 1¾ cups/9¼ oz flour, 1 tsp baking soda, 2 tsp vanilla, ½ cup oil, 1½ cups boiling water or coffee, 2 eggs, 2 cups Vanilla Cream), as do 350°F, upper-third rack, the 3-inch pan drop, the 25-to-30-minute bake, the 1 cup of cream between the layers, the up-to-2-hour chill, and the 4-days-room-temperature / 2-months-freezer keep. **[§8.37 Chicken and Garlic Soup](codys-cookbook.md#837-chicken-and-garlic-soup) (pp. 332–333): confirmed clean a second time**, matching the independent cross-check already logged — all ingredients, the 4-minutes-per-side sear, 12-minute vegetables, 20-second garlic bloom, 12-minute breast pull, 50-minute total, 5-day/2-month keep, and all four variations and serving tips. **No salt quantity was changed anywhere in this pass**, and nothing was invented to close a gap. |

the badge's prep note follows. **Toothpicks** stay on the ingredient list but are now marked as a Step 4 call, not a printed ingredient. **The ¼ cup kosher salt per pound is unchanged — no salt quantity was altered anywhere in this pass.** **[§7.116 Joe's Fireproof Grilled Shrimp](codys-cookbook.md#7116-joes-fireproof-grilled-shrimp): three fixes.** Start-to-finish corrected from ~50 min to **~50–60 min** (the page prints TAKES *50 to 60 minutes*); **"wild Gulf"** restored to the ingredient line and the grocery list, where the source specifies it outright rather than "preferably"; and the contributor credit corrected — the page prints Joe Parajecki as **"operation manager"** (not operations) and puts **Madison, Wisconsin** with Joe rather than with the Kettle Range Meat Company. Its MAKES *2 servings as a main dish* already matched. **[§T56 Shrimp: Sizing, Deveining, and Brining](codys-cookbook.md#t56-shrimp-sizing-deveining-and-brining): one correction and four recovered passages.** The brine soak is corrected to **about 20 minutes, but no longer** (it read 15 to 20). **Book p. 341 — the whole "Choosing and Prepping Shrimp" opener — had never made it into this cookbook at all** and is now added: most shrimp are farm raised in Asia amid unpleasant revelations about conditions there, get **wild Gulf shrimp if you can**, flash-frozen is usually high quality (defrost in the fridge), and so-called "fresh" shrimp has usually been frozen and thawed or traveled a long way. Three more p. 342 passages are restored: **cleaning whole head-on shrimp** (break the head off where the carapace meets the segmented abdomen, then rip off the legs), **the shell-eater's warning** (shells, swimmerets and tails are tasty and nutritious, but snip the point between the tail fins before cooking so it can't lodge in your throat), and the book's **chitin pronunciation, KITE-in**. The "16/20 are jumbos" line now also carries Meathead's own qualifier that he considers them large. **The ¼ cup salt / 1 cup warm / 2 cups cold water brine ratio was transcribed correctly the first time and is untouched.** **[§T57 Lobster: Choosing, Preparing, and Storing](codys-cookbook.md#t57-lobster-choosing-preparing-and-storing): verified correct as written, no changes.** Every claim on book pp. 344–345 checks out — the cold-water/warm-water contrast, the 1½-to-2-pound weights and sub-1-pound meat yield, the lively-vs-listless test, the green-algae aside, the rubber bands, grasp-from-above, and cook-the-same-day — as do all eight labels on the book's lobster diagram. Its **storing** paragraph comes from book p. 346, which is not in this photo set and so could not be re-checked; that is now stated in the entry rather than left implied. **The "Nesbitt, Missouri" flag is confirmed, not resolved:** read at full resolution, book p. 344 really does print **"Nesbitt, Missouri"** — double-**t** Nesbitt, and Missouri — for Melissa Cookston's Yazoo's Delta Q, which is in the real world Nesbit, **Mississippi**. It stays transcribed as printed with the flag intact. **Both recipes cite the correct book of the two Meathead titles in this cookbook** — *Meathead: The Science of Great Barbecue and Grilling*, not *The Meathead Method* — confirmed by the p. 342/344 "MEATHEAD" running footers. **Nothing was illegible and guessed at, and no recipe was deleted.** TOC annotations updated for both recipes. |

meat corrected to **1.5 kg (3.3 lb)**, tomatoes to **sieved tomatoes / passata di pomodoro, 1.5 kg (53 oz)**, and the nutmeg moved back to the tomato step where the source puts it. **[§7.86 Carpaccio of Sous Vide Octopus](codys-cookbook.md#786-carpaccio-of-sous-vide-octopus)** — small-print flag retired (the 2% transglutaminase ratio, the water-bottle forming method and the 4-hour firming were all confirmed exactly as printed), but the bath is **170°F (77°C)**, not 175°F, it **serves 6**, not 4, **1 carrot was missing entirely**, bay leaves are **2** and juniper berries **6**. **[§14.20 Cream Caramel](codys-cookbook.md#1420-cream-caramel)** — **every single quantity was wrong**: caramel sugar **¼ cup, not ¾ — three times the printed amount**, milk and cream **⅔ cup (150 ml) each**, custard sugar **60 g** (the "~87 g" was invented), moulds **½ cup (120 ml)**; the "4–6 hour chill" was not in the source at all (it prints a 10-minute bench rest, then a fridge set, made a day ahead), and the variation infuses **thirty minutes**, not a few.
